CONSOLIDATED TEXT

Last modification: April 28, 2023

JUAN CARLOS I

KING OF SPAIN

To all who see and understand this present.

Know ye: That the General Courts have approved and I hereby sanction the following

Organic Law:

EXPLANATORY MEMORANDUM

If the legal order has been defined as a set of norms that regulate the use of force, the importance of the Criminal Code in any civilized society can be easily understood. The Criminal Code defines the felonies and petty offenses that constitute the prerequisites for the application of the supreme form that the coercive power of the State may take: criminal punishment. Consequently, it occupies a preeminent place in the legal order as a whole, to the point that, not without reason, it has been considered as a kind of "negative Constitution." The Criminal Code must protect the basic values and principles of social coexistence. When those values and principles change, it must also change. In our country, however, despite the profound social, economic and political changes, the text in force dates, in what could be considered its basic core, from the last century. The need for its reform cannot, therefore, be disputed.

Based on the various reform attempts carried out since the establishment of the democratic regime, the Government has prepared the bill that it submits for discussion and approval by the Chambers. It must, therefore, set out, if only briefly, the criteria that inspire it, although these may be easily deduced from reading its text.

The axis of these criteria has been, as is logical, that of the positive adaptation of the new Criminal Code to constitutional values. The changes introduced in that direction by this bill are innumerable, but some are worth highlighting.

First, a total reform of the current system of penalties is proposed, so as to achieve, as far as possible, the objectives of resocialization assigned to it by the Constitution. The proposed system simplifies, on the one hand, the regulation of custodial penalties, while expanding the possibilities of replacing them with others that affect less basic legal rights, and, on the other hand, introduces changes in pecuniary penalties, adopting the day-fine system and adding community service.

Second, the antinomy between the principle of minimal intervention and the growing needs for protection in an increasingly complex society has been addressed, prudently accommodating new forms of crime, while eliminating criminal offenses that have lost their reason for being. In the first sense, the introduction of offenses against the socioeconomic order or the new regulation of offenses relating to land use planning and natural resources deserves mention; in the second, the disappearance of the complex forms of robbery with violence and intimidation against persons which, having arisen in the context of the fight against banditry, must disappear giving way to the application of general rules.

Third, special importance has been given to the protection of fundamental rights and care has been taken to design with special restraint the use of the punitive instrument where the exercise of any of them is at stake: as an example, on the one hand, the specific protection of moral integrity and, on the other, the new regulation of offenses against honor. By specifically protecting moral integrity, citizens are given stronger protection against torture, and by configuring offenses against honor in the manner proposed, freedom of expression is given all the relevance that a democratic regime can and should recognize.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

Fourth, and in line with the objective of protecting and respecting fundamental rights, the privileged regime from which illegitimate interference by public officials in the sphere of citizens' rights and freedoms has hitherto enjoyed has been eliminated. Therefore, it is proposed that detentions, entries and searches of homes carried out by an authority or official outside the cases permitted by law, be treated as aggravated forms of the corresponding common offenses, and not as they have been until now, that is, as special offenses incomprehensibly and unjustifiably mitigated.

Fifth, efforts have been made to advance on the path of real and effective equality, seeking to fulfill the task that, in that sense, the Constitution imposes on public authorities. Certainly, the Criminal Code is not the most important instrument to carry out that task; however, it can contribute to it, eliminating regulations that are an obstacle to its realization or introducing protective measures against discriminatory situations. In addition to the norms that grant specific protection against activities tending toward discrimination, mention must be made here of the new regulation of offenses against sexual freedom. Its purpose is to adapt the criminal types to the protected legal interest, which is no longer, as it was historically, the honesty of women, but the sexual freedom of all. Under the protection of women's honesty lay an intolerable situation of grievance, which the proposed regulation completely eliminates. The novelty of the punitive techniques used may be surprising; but, in this case, departing from tradition seems wise.

Leaving the realm of principles and descending to that of drafting techniques, this bill differs from previous ones in its claim to universality. The idea was that the Criminal Code should constitute a complete regulation of the punitive power of the State. The realization of this idea already started from a deficit, given the importance that the sanctioning power of the Administration has in our country; but, moreover, it was unnecessary and disturbing.

Unnecessary, because the nineteenth-century option in favor of the Criminal Code and against special laws was based on the undeniable fact that the legislator, when preparing a Code, was constrained, for external reasons of social significance, to respect constitutional principles, which did not occur, or occurred to a lesser extent, in the case of a particular law. In the framework of flexible constitutionalism, that was an argument of special importance to support the claim of absolute universality of the Code. Today, however, both the Criminal Code and special laws are hierarchically subordinated to the Constitution and obliged to submit to it, not only because of that hierarchy, but also because of the existence of judicial review of constitutionality. Consequently, special laws cannot raise the mistrust they historically provoked.

Disturbing, because, although it is undeniable that a Code would not deserve that name if it did not contain most of the criminal norms and, of course, the basic informing principles of all regulation, the truth is that there are matters that can hardly be introduced into it. For, if a relative claim to universality is inherent in the idea of Code, so are those of stability and permanence, and there are areas in which, due to the special situation of the rest of the legal order, or due to the very nature of things, that stability and permanence are impossible. Such is, for example, the case of offenses relating to exchange controls. In them, the constant modification of economic conditions and the normative context, in which, whether one likes it or not, such offenses are integrated, advises placing the criminal norms in that context and leaving them outside the Code: moreover, that is our tradition, and there is no lack, in the countries around us, of characterized examples of similar proceedings.

Thus, in that and other similar cases, the option has been to refer to the corresponding special laws the criminal regulation of the respective matters. The same technique has been used for the rules governing the decriminalization of voluntary termination of pregnancy. In this case, along with reasons similar to those previously stated, it could be argued that these are not incriminating norms, but norms that regulate cases of non-incrimination. The Constitutional Court required that, in the configuration of such cases, guarantees be adopted that do not seem proper to a Criminal Code, but rather to another type of norm.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

In the preparation of the bill, the parliamentary discussions of 1992, the opinion of the General Council of the Judiciary, the state of jurisprudence and the opinions of scientific doctrine have been taken very much into account. It has been carried out from the deeply felt idea that the Criminal Code must belong to everyone and that, consequently, all opinions must be heard and the most reasonable solutions chosen, that is, those that everyone should be able to accept. The claim is not to have produced a perfect work, but simply a useful work. The Government does not have the last word here, but only the first. It limits itself, therefore, with this bill, to pronouncing it, inviting all political forces and all citizens to collaborate in the task of its improvement. Only if we all wish to have a better Criminal Code and contribute to achieving it will it be possible to achieve an objective whose importance for coexistence and the peaceful enjoyment of the rights and freedoms proclaimed by the Constitution could hardly be exaggerated.

PRELIMINARY TITLE

On criminal guarantees and the application of criminal law

###### Article 1.

1. No action or omission that is not provided for as an offense by law prior to its commission shall be punished.

2. Security measures may only be applied when the prerequisites previously established by Law are met.

###### Article 2.

1. No offense shall be punished with a penalty that is not provided for by law prior to its commission. Laws establishing security measures shall likewise lack retroactive effect.

2. Notwithstanding, those criminal laws that favor the defendant shall have retroactive effect, even if a final judgment has been rendered and the subject is serving sentence when they enter into force. In case of doubt about determining the most favorable Law, the defendant shall be heard. Acts committed under the validity of a temporary Law shall, however, be judged in accordance with it, unless expressly provided otherwise.

###### Article 3.

1. No penalty or security measure may be executed except by virtue of a final judgment issued by the competent Judge or Court, in accordance with procedural laws.

2. Neither may any penalty or security measure be executed in any form other than that prescribed by Law and the regulations that implement it, nor with circumstances or incidents other than those expressed in its text. The execution of the penalty or security measure shall be carried out under the control of the competent Judges and Courts.

###### Article 4.

1. Criminal laws shall not be applied to cases other than those expressly included in them.

2. In the event that a Judge or Court, in the exercise of its jurisdiction, becomes aware of any action or omission that, without being penalized by Law, it deems worthy of punishment, it shall refrain from all proceedings concerning it and shall present to the Government the reasons supporting its belief that it should be subject to criminal sanction.

3. In the same manner, it shall approach the Government presenting what is appropriate regarding the repeal or modification of the provision or the granting of pardon, without prejudice to immediately executing the sentence, when from the rigorous application of the provisions of Law an action or omission is penalized that, in the judgment of the Judge or Court, should not be, or when the penalty is notably excessive, considering the harm caused by the offense and the personal circumstances of the defendant.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

4. If a petition for pardon is made, and the Judge or Court has determined in a reasoned decision that the right to a trial without undue delay may be violated by serving the sentence, it shall suspend its execution until the petition filed is resolved.

The Judge or Court may also suspend the execution of the sentence, while the pardon is not resolved, when, if the sentence were executed, the purpose thereof could become illusory.

###### Article 5.

There is no penalty without intent or negligence.

###### Article 6.

1. Security measures are based on the criminal dangerousness of the subject on whom they are imposed, manifested in the commission of an act provided for as an offense.

2. Security measures cannot be more onerous or of longer duration than the penalty abstractly applicable to the act committed, nor exceed the limit of what is necessary to prevent the dangerousness of the perpetrator.

###### Article 7.

For the purposes of determining the applicable criminal law in time, offenses are considered committed at the moment when the subject executes the action or omits the act that he was obliged to perform.

###### Article 8.

Acts capable of being classified according to two or more provisions of this Code, and not included in articles 73 to 77, shall be punished observing the following rules:

1.ª The special provision shall be applied in preference to the general.

2.ª The subsidiary provision shall be applied only in default of the principal, whether such subsidiarity is expressly declared or is tacitly deducible.

3.ª The broader or more complex criminal provision shall absorb those that punish the offenses subsumed in it.

4.ª In default of the foregoing criteria, the most serious criminal provision shall exclude those that punish the act with a lesser penalty.

###### Article 9.

The provisions of this Title shall apply to offenses that are penalized by special laws. The remaining provisions of this Code shall apply as supplementary in matters not expressly provided for by them.

## BOOK I

General provisions on offenses, responsible persons, penalties, security measures and other consequences of criminal offenses.

#### TITLE I

On criminal offenses

##### CHAPTER I

On offenses

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Article 10.

Offenses are intentional or negligent actions and omissions penalized by law.

###### Article 11.

Offenses consisting in the production of a result shall only be understood as committed by omission when the failure to prevent it, by violating a special legal duty of the perpetrator, is equivalent, according to the meaning of the text of the law, to causing it. For this purpose, omission shall be equated to action:

- a) When there is a specific legal or contractual obligation to act.
- b) When the person who omits has created an occasion of risk for the legally protected interest through a preceding action or omission.

###### Article 12.

Negligent actions or omissions shall only be punished when expressly provided by Law.

###### Article 13.

1. Serious offenses are infractions that the Law punishes with a serious penalty.

2. Less serious offenses are infractions that the Law punishes with a less serious penalty.

3. Minor offenses are infractions that the law punishes with a minor penalty.

4. When the penalty, by its extent, can be included simultaneously among those mentioned in the first two paragraphs of this article, the offense shall be considered, in any case, as serious. When the penalty, by its extent, can be considered as minor and as less serious, the offense shall be considered, in any case, as minor.

###### Article 14.

1. Invincible mistake about a fact constituting a criminal offense excludes criminal liability. If the mistake, considering the circumstances of the fact and the personal circumstances of the perpetrator, were vincible, the offense shall be punished, where appropriate, as negligent.

2. Mistake about a fact that qualifies the offense or about an aggravating circumstance shall prevent its appreciation.

3. Invincible mistake about the unlawfulness of the act constituting the criminal offense excludes criminal liability. If the mistake were vincible, the penalty one or two degrees lower shall be applied.

###### Article 15.

Completed offenses and attempts to commit offenses are punishable.

###### Article 16.

1. There is an attempt when the subject begins the execution of the offense directly by external acts, performing all or part of the acts that objectively should produce the result, and yet it does not occur due to causes independent of the perpetrator's will.

2. Whoever voluntarily prevents the completion of the offense, either by desisting from the execution already begun or by preventing the production of the result, shall be exempt from criminal liability for the attempted offense, without prejudice to the liability that may have been incurred for the acts executed, if these already constitute another offense.

3. When several subjects intervene in an act, he or those who desist from the execution already begun and prevent or attempt to prevent, seriously, firmly and decisively, the completion, shall be exempt from criminal liability, without prejudice to the liability they may have incurred for the acts executed, if these already constitute another offense.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Article 17.

1. Conspiracy exists when two or more persons agree to commit a crime and resolve to carry it out.

2. Solicitation exists when a person who has resolved to commit a crime invites another person or persons to participate in it.

3. Conspiracy and solicitation to commit a crime shall only be punished in cases specifically provided for by law.

###### Article 18.

1. Provocation exists when direct incitement to commit a crime is made through the press, broadcasting or any other similarly effective means that facilitates publicity, or before a gathering of persons.

Apology, for the purposes of this Code, is the exposition, before a gathering of persons or through any means of dissemination, of ideas or doctrines that glorify crime or exalt its author. Apology shall only be criminal as a form of provocation and if by its nature and circumstances it constitutes a direct incitement to commit a crime.

2. Provocation shall be punished exclusively in cases where the Law so provides. If the provocation has been followed by the commission of the crime, it shall be punished as inducement.

##### CHAPTER II

Grounds for exemption from criminal liability

###### Article 19.

Persons under eighteen years of age shall not be criminally liable under this Code.

When a minor of such age commits a criminal act, he or she may be held liable in accordance with the provisions of the law governing criminal liability of minors.

###### Article 20.

The following are exempt from criminal liability:

1.º A person who, at the time of committing the criminal offense, due to any psychological abnormality or disorder, is unable to understand the unlawfulness of the act or to act in accordance with such understanding.

Transitory mental disorder shall not exempt from punishment when it has been provoked by the subject for the purpose of committing the crime or when he or she has foreseen or should have foreseen its commission. 2.º A person who, at the time of committing the criminal offense, is in a state of full intoxication due to the consumption of alcoholic beverages, toxic drugs, narcotics, psychotropic substances or others producing similar effects, provided that such state has not been sought for the purpose of committing it or that its commission has not been foreseen or should not have been foreseen, or is under the influence of a withdrawal syndrome, due to dependence on such substances, that prevents him or her from understanding the unlawfulness of the act or acting in accordance with such understanding.

3.º A person who, by suffering alterations in perception from birth or from childhood, has a severely altered awareness of reality.

4.º A person who acts in defense of his or her own or others' person or rights, provided that the following requirements are met:

First. Unlawful aggression. In the case of defense of property, an attack on the same that constitutes a crime and puts it in grave danger of imminent damage or loss shall be deemed unlawful aggression. In the case of defense of the dwelling or its appurtenances, unlawful entry into the same shall be deemed unlawful aggression.

Second. Rational necessity of the means employed to prevent or repel it.

Third. Lack of sufficient provocation on the part of the defender.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

5.º A person who, in a state of necessity, in order to avoid harm to himself or herself or to another, injures a legal interest of another person or breaches a duty, provided that the following requirements are met: First. That the harm caused is not greater than that which is to be avoided.

Second. That the situation of necessity has not been intentionally provoked by the subject.

Third. That the person in need does not have, by virtue of his or her profession or position, an obligation to sacrifice himself or herself. 6.º A person who acts impelled by insurmountable fear.

7.º A person who acts in fulfillment of a duty or in the legitimate exercise of a right, profession or position.

In the cases provided for in the first three numbers, security measures provided for in this Code shall be applied, where appropriate.

##### CHAPTER III

Circumstances that mitigate criminal liability

###### Article 21.

The following are mitigating circumstances:

1.ª The grounds set forth in the preceding chapter, when not all the requirements necessary to exempt from liability in their respective cases are met. 2.ª The offender acting due to his or her serious addiction to the substances mentioned in number 2.º of the preceding article.

3.ª Acting due to causes or stimuli so powerful that they have produced a fit of passion, obsession or other emotional state of similar nature.

4.ª The offender having proceeded, before learning that judicial proceedings are being brought against him or her, to confess the offense to the authorities.

5.ª The offender having proceeded to repair the harm caused to the victim, or to diminish its effects, at any time during the proceedings and prior to the holding of the oral trial.

6.ª Extraordinary and undue delay in the processing of the proceedings, provided that it is not attributable to the accused himself or herself and is not proportionate to the complexity of the case.

7.ª Any other circumstance of analogous significance to the foregoing.

##### CHAPTER IV

Circumstances that aggravate criminal liability

###### Article 22.

The following are aggravating circumstances:

1.ª Committing the act with treachery.

There is treachery when the offender commits any of the crimes against persons employing in the execution means, methods or forms that tend directly or especially to ensure it, without the risk to his or her person that might arise from defense by the victim.

2.ª Committing the act by means of disguise, with abuse of superiority or taking advantage of circumstances of place, time or assistance of other persons that weaken the defense of the victim or facilitate the impunity of the offender.

3.ª Committing the act for a price, reward or promise.

4.ª Committing the crime for racist, anti-Semitic, anti-Gypsy or other discriminatory reasons relating to the ideology, religion or beliefs of the victim, the ethnicity, race or nation to which he or she belongs, his or her sex, age, sexual orientation or gender identity, reasons of gender, aporophobia or social exclusion, the illness he or she suffers from or his or her

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

disability, regardless of whether such conditions or circumstances actually exist in the person upon whom the conduct falls.

5.ª Deliberately and inhumanly increasing the suffering of the victim, causing the victim unnecessary pain for the commission of the crime.

6.ª Acting with abuse of trust.

7.ª Taking advantage of the public character of the offender.

8.ª Being a recidivist.

There is recidivism when, upon offending, the offender has been finally convicted of a crime included in the same title of this Code, provided that it is of the same nature.

For the purposes of this number, criminal records that have been expunged or should be expunged, or those corresponding to minor offenses, shall not be counted.

Final convictions by judges or courts imposed in other States of the European Union shall produce the effects of recidivism unless the criminal record has been expunged or could be expunged in accordance with Spanish law.

##### CHAPTER V

The mixed circumstance of kinship

###### Article 23.

It is a circumstance that may mitigate or aggravate liability, depending on the nature, motives and effects of the crime, that the aggrieved party is or has been the spouse or a person who is or has been bound in a stable relationship of analogous affection, or is an ascendant, descendant or sibling by birth or adoption of the offender or of his or her spouse or cohabitant.

##### CHAPTER VI

General provisions

###### Article 24.

1. For criminal purposes, a person who alone or as a member of a corporation, court or collegiate body has command or exercises jurisdiction shall be deemed an authority. In any case, members of the Congress of Deputies, the Senate, the Legislative Assemblies of the Autonomous Communities and the European Parliament shall be considered authorities. Officials of the Public Prosecutor's Office and Prosecutors of the European Public Prosecutor's Office shall also be considered authorities.

2. Any person who by immediate provision of Law or by election or by appointment of competent authority participates in the exercise of public functions shall be considered a public official.

###### Article 25.

For the purposes of this Code, disability is understood as that situation in which a person finds themselves with permanent physical, mental, intellectual or sensory impairments which, when interacting with various barriers, may limit or impede their full and effective participation in society, on equal terms with others. Likewise, for the purposes of this Code, a person with disability in need of special protection shall be understood as that person with disability who, whether or not their capacity to act has been judicially modified, requires assistance or support for the exercise of their legal capacity and for decision-making regarding their person, their rights or interests due to their permanent intellectual or mental impairments.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

###### Article 26.

For the purposes of this Code, a document is considered any material medium that expresses or incorporates data, facts or narrations with evidentiary efficacy or any other type of legal relevance.

#### TITLE II

Of persons criminally liable for offenses

###### Article 27.

The perpetrators and accomplices are criminally liable for offenses.

###### Article 28.

Perpetrators are those who commit the act by themselves alone, jointly or through another whom they use as an instrument.

The following shall also be considered perpetrators:

- a) Those who directly induce another or others to commit it.
- b) Those who cooperate in its commission with an act without which it would not have been carried out.

###### Article 29.

Accomplices are those who, not being included in the preceding article, cooperate in the commission of the act with prior or simultaneous acts.

###### Article 30.

1. In offenses committed using mechanical means or media of dissemination, neither accomplices nor those who have favored them personally or materially shall be criminally liable.

2. The perpetrators referred to in Article 28 shall be liable in a graduated, exclusive and subsidiary manner according to the following order:

1.º Those who actually drafted the text or produced the sign in question, and those who induced them to do so.

2.º The directors of the publication or program in which it is disseminated.

3.º The directors of the publishing, broadcasting or disseminating company.

4.º The directors of the recording, reproduction or printing company.

3. When for any reason other than the extinction of criminal liability, including a declaration of fugitive status or residence outside Spain, none of the persons included in any of the numbers of the preceding paragraph can be prosecuted, the proceedings shall be directed against those mentioned in the immediately following number.

###### Article 31.

A person who acts as a de facto or de jure administrator of a legal entity, or in the name or legal or voluntary representation of another, shall be personally liable, even if they do not possess the conditions, qualities or relations that the corresponding offense requires to be its active subject, if such circumstances exist in the entity or person in whose name or on whose behalf they act.

###### Article 31 bis.

1. In the cases provided for in this Code, legal persons shall be criminally liable:

- a) For crimes committed in the name or on behalf of the same, and for their direct or indirect benefit, by their legal representatives or by those who, acting individually or as members of a body of the legal person, are authorized LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA to make decisions in the name of the legal person or hold powers of organization and control within the same.
- b) For crimes committed, in the exercise of corporate activities and on behalf of and for the direct or indirect benefit of the same, by those who, being subject to the authority of the natural persons mentioned in the preceding paragraph, have been able to carry out the acts due to serious breach by those persons of the duties of supervision, monitoring and control of their activity, taking into account the specific circumstances of the case.
  - 2. If the crime were committed by the persons indicated in letter a) of the preceding section, the legal person shall be exempt from liability if the following conditions are met:

1.ª the governing body has adopted and effectively implemented, before the commission of the crime, organization and management models that include suitable monitoring and control measures to prevent crimes of the same nature or to significantly reduce the risk of their commission;

2.ª supervision of the operation and compliance with the prevention model implemented has been entrusted to a body of the legal person with autonomous powers of initiative and control or that has been legally assigned the function of supervising the effectiveness of the internal controls of the legal person;

3.ª the individual perpetrators have committed the crime by fraudulently evading the organization and prevention models and

4.ª there has been no omission or insufficient exercise of its supervision, monitoring and control functions by the body referred to in condition 2.ª In cases where the foregoing circumstances can only be partially proven, this circumstance shall be assessed for the purposes of mitigation of the penalty.

3. In small legal persons, the supervision functions referred to in condition 2.ª of section 2 may be assumed directly by the governing body. For these purposes, small legal persons are those that, according to applicable legislation, are authorized to present an abbreviated profit and loss statement.

4. If the crime were committed by the persons indicated in letter b) of section 1, the legal person shall be exempt from liability if, before the commission of the crime, it has adopted and effectively implemented an organization and management model that is adequate to prevent crimes of the nature of that which was committed or to significantly reduce the risk of their commission.

In this case, the mitigation provided for in the second paragraph of section 2 of this article shall be equally applicable.

5. The organization and management models referred to in condition 1.ª of section 2 and the preceding section must meet the following requirements:

1.º They shall identify the activities within whose scope the crimes that must be prevented may be committed.

2.º They shall establish the protocols or procedures that specify the process of formation of the will of the legal person, of decision-making and of execution thereof in relation to those.

3.º They shall have financial resource management models adequate to prevent the commission of the crimes that must be prevented.

4.º They shall impose the obligation to report possible risks and breaches to the body responsible for monitoring the operation and observance of the prevention model. 5.º They shall establish a disciplinary system that adequately sanctions non-compliance with the measures established by the model.

6.º They shall carry out a periodic verification of the model and its possible modification when relevant violations of its provisions become evident, or when changes occur in the organization, in the control structure or in the activity carried out that make them necessary.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Article 31 ter.

1. The criminal liability of legal persons shall be enforceable whenever the commission of a crime is established that must have been committed by someone holding the positions or functions referred to in the preceding article, even when the specific natural person responsible has not been identified or it has not been possible to direct the proceedings against them. When as a consequence of the same facts both are imposed a fine, the judges or courts shall modulate the respective amounts, so that the resulting sum is not disproportionate in relation to the seriousness of those facts.

2. The concurrence, in the persons who have materially carried out the acts or in those who have made them possible by not having exercised due control, of circumstances that affect the culpability of the accused or aggravate their liability, or the fact that said persons have died or have evaded justice, shall not exclude or modify the criminal liability of legal persons, without prejudice to what is provided in the following article.

###### Article 31 quater.

1. Only the following activities, carried out after the commission of the crime and through their legal representatives, may be considered mitigating circumstances of the criminal liability of legal persons:

- a) Having proceeded, before learning that the judicial proceedings are directed against it, to confess the offense to the authorities.
- b) Having collaborated in the investigation of the act by providing evidence, at any moment of the process, that was new and decisive for clarifying the criminal liabilities arising from the facts.
- c) Having proceeded at any moment of the proceedings and prior to the oral trial to repair or reduce the damage caused by the crime.
- d) Having established, before the commencement of the oral trial, effective measures to prevent and discover crimes that in the future could be committed with the means or under the cover of the legal person.

###### Article 31 quinquies.

1. The provisions relating to the criminal liability of legal persons shall not be applicable to the State, to territorial and institutional public Administrations, to Regulatory Bodies, public Business Agencies and Entities, to international organizations of public law, nor to those others that exercise public powers of sovereignty or administrative powers.

2. In the case of public commercial companies that execute public policies or provide services of general economic interest, only the penalties provided for in letters a) and g) of section 7 of article 33 may be imposed on them. This limitation shall not be applicable when the judge or court determines that it is a legal form created by its promoters, founders, administrators or representatives with the purpose of evading possible criminal liability.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

#### TITLE III

Of penalties

##### CHAPTER I

Of penalties, their classes and effects

###### Section 1.ª Of penalties and their classes

###### Article 32.

The penalties that may be imposed under this Code, either as principal or as accessory, are deprivation of liberty, deprivation of other rights and fine.

###### Artículo 33.

1. Based on their nature and duration, penalties are classified as serious, less serious, and minor.

2. Serious penalties are:

- a) Reviewable permanent imprisonment.
- b) Imprisonment exceeding five years.
- c) Absolute disqualification.
- d) Special disqualifications for a period exceeding five years.
- e) Suspension from employment or public office for a period exceeding five years.
- f) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds for a period exceeding eight years.
- g) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons for a period exceeding eight years.
- h) Deprivation of the right to reside in or go to certain places, for a period exceeding five years.
- i) Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period exceeding five years.
- j) Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period exceeding five years.
- k) Deprivation of parental authority.
  - 3. Less serious penalties are:
- a) Imprisonment from three months to five years.
- b) Special disqualifications up to five years.
- c) Suspension from employment or public office up to five years.
- d) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from one year and one day to eight years.
- e) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from one year and one day to eight years.
- f) Special disqualification for the practice of a profession, trade or business related to animals and for the possession of animals from one year and one day to five years.
- g) Deprivation of the right to reside in or go to certain places, for a period from six months to five years.
- h) Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period from six months to five years.
- i) Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period from six months to five years.
- j) A fine of more than three months.
- k) Proportional fine, whatever its amount, except as provided in paragraph 7 of this article.
- l) Community service from thirty-one days to one year.
  - 4. Minor penalties are:

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

a) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds from three months to one year.

b) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons from three months to one year.

c) Special disqualification for the practice of a profession, trade or business related to animals and for the possession of animals from three months to one year.

d) Deprivation of the right to reside in or go to certain places, for a period of less than six months.

e) Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period from one month to less than six months.

f) Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, for a period from one month to less than six months.

g) A fine of up to three months.

h) Permanent location monitoring from one day to three months.

i) Community service from one to thirty days.

5. Subsidiary personal liability for non-payment of a fine shall be of a less serious or minor nature, depending on that corresponding to the penalty it replaces.

6. Accessory penalties shall have the same duration as the principal penalty, except as expressly provided by other provisions of this Code.

7. The penalties applicable to legal persons, all of which are considered serious, are as follows:

- a) Fine by installments or proportional.
- b) Dissolution of the legal person. Dissolution shall result in the definitive loss of its legal personality, as well as its capacity to act in any way in legal transactions, or to carry out any kind of activity, even if lawful.
- c) Suspension of its activities for a period that may not exceed five years.
- d) Closure of its premises and establishments for a period that may not exceed five years.
- e) Prohibition from carrying out in the future the activities in the exercise of which the offense was committed, facilitated or concealed. This prohibition may be temporary or permanent. If temporary, the period may not exceed fifteen years.
- f) Disqualification from obtaining subsidies and public aid, from contracting with the public sector and from enjoying tax or Social Security benefits and incentives, for a period that may not exceed fifteen years.
- g) Judicial intervention to safeguard the rights of workers or creditors for the time deemed necessary, which may not exceed five years.

The intervention may affect the entire organization or be limited to some of its facilities, sections or business units. The Judge or Court, in the judgment or, subsequently, by order, shall determine exactly the content of the intervention and shall determine who will be in charge of the intervention and within what time frames they must produce monitoring reports for the judicial body. The intervention may be modified or suspended at any time following a report from the receiver and the Public Prosecutor's Office. The receiver shall have the right to access all facilities and premises of the company or legal person and to receive whatever information they deem necessary for the exercise of their functions. The aspects related to the exercise of the receiver's function, such as remuneration or necessary qualifications, shall be determined by regulation.

The temporary closure of premises or establishments, the suspension of corporate activities and judicial intervention may also be ordered by the Examining Judge as a precautionary measure during the investigation of the case.

###### Artículo 34.

The following shall not be deemed penalties:

- 1. Detention and preventive imprisonment and other precautionary measures of a criminal nature.
- 2. Fines and other corrections that, in the exercise of governmental or disciplinary powers, are imposed on subordinates or those under administration.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

3. Deprivations of rights and reparatory sanctions established by civil or administrative laws.

###### Sección 2.ª On custodial penalties

###### Artículo 35.

Custodial penalties are reviewable permanent imprisonment, imprisonment, permanent location monitoring and subsidiary personal liability for non-payment of a fine. Their enforcement, as well as penitentiary benefits that involve shortening of the sentence, shall conform to the provisions of the laws and this Code.

###### Artículo 36.

1. The penalty of permanent imprisonment shall be reviewed in accordance with the provisions of

###### article 92.

The classification of the convicted person in the third grade must be authorized by the court after an individualized and favorable prognosis of social reintegration, having heard the Public Prosecutor's Office and Penitentiary Institutions, and may not be carried out:

- a) Until completion of twenty years of actual imprisonment, in the event that the convicted person had been convicted of an offence under Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code.
- b) Until completion of fifteen years of actual imprisonment, in all other cases.

In these cases, the convicted person may not enjoy leave permits until having served a minimum of twelve years of imprisonment, in the case provided for in letter a), and eight years of imprisonment, in that provided for in letter b).

2. The prison sentence shall have a minimum duration of three months and a maximum of twenty years, except as exceptionally provided by other provisions of this Code. When the duration of the prison sentence imposed is greater than five years, the judge or court may order that the classification of the convicted person in the third grade of penitentiary treatment not be carried out until completion of half of the sentence imposed. In any case, when the duration of the prison sentence imposed is greater than five years and it concerns the offences listed below, the classification of the convicted person in the third grade of penitentiary treatment may not be carried out until completion of half thereof:

- a) Offences relating to terrorist organizations and groups and offences of terrorism under

##### Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code.

b) Offences committed within a criminal organization or group.

c) Offences under Title VII bis of Book II of this Code, where the victim is a minor or a person with a disability in need of special protection.

d) Offences under article 181.

e) Offences under Chapter V of Title VIII of Book II of this Code, where the victim is under sixteen years of age.

In the cases under letters c), d) and e), if the sentence is greater than five years of imprisonment, the classification of the convicted person in the third grade of penitentiary treatment may not be carried out without specific assessment and report on the offender's use of the treatment program for those convicted of sexual assault.

3. The judicial authority for penitentiary supervision, after an individualized and favorable prognosis of social reintegration and assessing, as appropriate, the personal circumstances of the convicted person and the evolution of the re-educational treatment, may decide with reasons, having heard the Public Prosecutor's Office, Penitentiary Institutions and the other parties, the application of the general regime of compliance, except in the cases contained in the preceding paragraph.

4. In any case, the judicial authority for penitentiary supervision, as appropriate, may decide, after report from the Public Prosecutor's Office, Penitentiary Institutions and the other parties, progression to third grade for humanitarian reasons and personal dignity of convicted persons who are very seriously ill with incurable suffering and of septuagenarian persons, assessing, in particular, their low dangerousness.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

###### Article 37.

1. Permanent location shall have a duration of up to six months. Its compliance obliges the convicted person to remain at home or at a specific place fixed by the Judge in the judgment or subsequently by reasoned order.

However, in cases where permanent location is provided for as a principal penalty, having regard to the repetition in the commission of the offence and provided that the specific applicable provision so expressly provides, the Judge may decide in the judgment that the penalty of permanent location be served on Saturdays, Sundays and public holidays at the penitentiary center closest to the convicted person's home

2. If the offender so requests and circumstances so advise, having heard the public prosecutor, the sentencing judge or court may decide that the sentence be served on Saturdays and Sundays or in a non-continuous manner.

3. If the convicted person fails to comply with the penalty, the sentencing judge or court shall issue testimony to proceed in accordance with the provisions of article 468.

4. To guarantee effective compliance, the Judge or Court may order the use of mechanical or electronic means that allow the location of the offender.

###### Article 38.

1. When the offender is imprisoned, the duration of the sentences shall begin to be counted from the day on which the conviction has become final.

2. When the offender is not imprisoned, the duration of the sentences shall begin to be counted from the moment of admission to the appropriate establishment for their service.

###### Section 3 Penalties depriving of rights

###### Article 39.

The following are penalties depriving of rights:

- a) Absolute disqualification.
- b) Special disqualification for public employment or office, profession, trade, industry or commerce, or other activities, whether remunerated or not, or from the rights of parental authority, guardianship, care or curatorship, possession of animals, right of passive suffrage or any other right.
- c) Suspension from public employment or office.
- d) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds.
- e) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons.
- f) Deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or to go to them.
- g) Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court.
- h) Prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court.
- i) Community service work.
- j) Deprivation of parental authority.

###### Article 40.

1. The penalty of absolute disqualification shall have a duration of six to 20 years; those of special disqualification, from three months to 20 years, and that of suspension from public employment or office, from three months to six years.

2. The penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds, and that of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons, shall have a duration of three months to 10 years.

3. The penalty of deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or to go to them shall have a duration of up to 10 years. The prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons, or from communicating with them, shall have a duration of one month to 10 years.

4. The penalty of community service work shall have a duration of one day to one year.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

5. The duration of each of these penalties shall be as provided in the preceding paragraphs, except as exceptionally provided by other provisions of this Code.

###### Article 41.

The penalty of absolute disqualification produces the definitive deprivation of all honors, public employments and offices held by the convicted person, even if they are elective. It also produces incapacity to obtain them or any other honors, offices or public employments, and to be elected to public office, for the duration of the sentence.

###### Article 42.

The penalty of special disqualification for public employment or office produces the definitive deprivation of the employment or office upon which it falls, even if elective, and of the honors attached thereto. It also produces incapacity to obtain the same or similar ones, for the duration of the sentence. The judgment must specify the employments, offices and honors upon which the disqualification falls.

###### Article 43.

Suspension from public employment or office deprives the convicted person of its exercise for the duration of the sentence.

###### Article 44.

Special disqualification for the right of passive suffrage deprives the convicted person, for the duration of the sentence, of the right to be elected to public office.

###### Artículo 45.

Special disqualification for profession, trade, industry or commerce or other activities, whether or not remunerated, or any other right, which must be specified expressly and with reasoning in the sentence, deprives the convicted person of the power to exercise them during the term of the sentence. The judicial authority may restrict the disqualification to certain activities or functions of the profession or trade, remunerated or not, allowing, if possible, the exercise of those functions not directly related to the crime committed.

###### Artículo 46.

Special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, custody or foster care, deprives the convicted person of the rights inherent to the first, and entails the extinction of the others, as well as the incapacity to obtain appointment to such positions during the term of the sentence. The penalty of deprivation of parental authority implies the loss of its ownership, with those rights of which the son or daughter is the holder with respect to the convicted person that are judicially determined subsisting. The judicial authority may impose these penalties with respect to all or some of the minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who are in the care of the convicted person.

To specify which rights of minors or persons with disabilities must subsist in case of deprivation of parental authority and to determine with respect to which persons the penalty is imposed, the judicial authority shall assess the best interest of the minor or person with disability, in relation to the circumstances of the specific case.

For the purposes of this article, parental authority includes both that regulated in the Civil Code, including extended and rehabilitated authority, as well as analogous institutions provided for in the civil legislation of the autonomous communities.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Artículo 47.

The imposition of the penalty of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds shall disqualify the convicted person from exercising both rights during the time fixed in the sentence.

The imposition of the penalty of deprivation of the right to possession and carrying of weapons shall disqualify the convicted person from exercising this right for the time fixed in the sentence. When the penalty imposed is for a period exceeding two years, it shall entail the loss of validity of the permit or license enabling driving or possession and carrying, respectively.

###### Artículo 48.

1. The deprivation of the right to reside in or go to certain places prevents the convicted person from residing in or going to the place where the crime was committed, or to where the victim or their family resides, if they are different. In cases where there is a declared intellectual disability or a disability originating from a mental disorder, the specific case shall be studied in order to resolve bearing in mind the legal interests to be protected and the best interest of the person with disability who, as the case may be, must have the necessary means of accompaniment and support for compliance with the measure.

2. The prohibition from approaching the victim, or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, prevents the convicted person from approaching them, wherever they may be, as well as from approaching their home, their places of work and any other place frequented by them, with the visitation, communication and stay regime that, as the case may be, has been recognized in a civil judgment remaining suspended with respect to children until the full completion of this penalty.

3. The prohibition from communicating with the victim, or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, prevents the convicted person from establishing with them, by any means of communication or computer or telematic means, written, verbal or visual contact.

4. The judge or court may agree that the monitoring of these measures be carried out through electronic means that allow it.

###### Artículo 49.

Community service, which may not be imposed without the consent of the convicted person, obliges them to provide their unpaid cooperation in certain activities of public utility, which may consist, in relation to crimes of a similar nature to that committed by the convicted person, of work to repair the damage caused or to support or assist victims, as well as the participation of the convicted person in workshops or training programs for re-education, work, culture, road safety, sexual education, peaceful conflict resolution, positive parenting and other similar programs. Their daily duration may not exceed eight hours and their conditions shall be as follows:

1.ª Execution shall take place under the supervision of the Prison Supervision Judge, who, for this purpose, shall require reports on the performance of the work from the Administration, public entity or association of general interest in which the services are provided.

2.ª It shall not violate the dignity of the convicted person.

3.ª Community service shall be facilitated by the Administration, which may establish appropriate agreements for this purpose.

4.ª It shall enjoy the protection provided to convicted persons by penitentiary legislation regarding Social Security.

5.ª It shall not be subject to the achievement of economic interests.

6.ª The penitentiary social services, having made the necessary verifications, shall communicate to the Prison Supervision Judge the relevant incidents in the execution of the penalty and, in any case, if the convicted person:

- a) Is absent from work for at least two working days, provided that this constitutes a voluntary refusal on their part to comply with the penalty.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

b) Despite the requirements of the person in charge of the work center, their performance is significantly below the minimum required.

c) Opposes or repeatedly and manifestly fails to comply with the instructions given by the person in charge of the activity regarding its performance.

d) For any other reason, their conduct is such that the person in charge of the work refuses to continue maintaining them at the center.

Once the report has been assessed, the Prison Supervision Judge may agree to its execution at the same center, send the convicted person to complete its execution at another center, or determine that the convicted person has failed to comply with the penalty.

In case of non-compliance, testimony shall be deduced to proceed in accordance with

###### artículo 468.

7.ª If the convicted person is absent from work for justifiable cause, it shall not be understood as abandonment of the activity. However, the work lost shall not be counted in the settlement of the sentence, in which the days or working days actually worked out of the total imposed must be recorded.

###### Sección 4.ª Of the fine penalty

###### Artículo 50.

1. The fine penalty shall consist of the imposition on the convicted person of a pecuniary sanction.

2. The fine penalty shall be imposed, unless the Law provides otherwise, by the day-fine system.

3. Its minimum duration shall be ten days and the maximum two years. Fine penalties imposable on legal persons shall have a maximum duration of five years.

4. The daily quota shall have a minimum of two and a maximum of 400 euros, except in the case of fines imposable on legal persons, in which the daily quota shall have a minimum of 30 and a maximum of 5,000 euros. For calculation purposes, when the duration is set by months or years, months shall be understood to be thirty days and years three hundred sixty days.

5. The Judges or Courts shall determine with reasons the duration of the penalty within the limits established for each offense and according to the rules of chapter II of this Title. Likewise, they shall set in the sentence the amount of these quotas, taking into account exclusively the economic situation of the offender, deduced from their assets, income, obligations and family burdens and other personal circumstances.

6. The court, for justified cause, may authorize payment of the fine within a period not exceeding two years from the finality of the sentence, either in one payment or in installments to be determined. In this case, non-payment of two of them shall determine the maturity of the remaining ones.

###### Artículo 51.

If, after the sentence, the economic situation of the convicted person changes, the judge or court, exceptionally and after due investigation of such situation, may modify both the amount of the periodic quotas and the deadlines for their payment.

###### Artículo 52.

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding articles and when the Code so determines, the fine shall be established in proportion to the damage caused, the value of the object of the offense or the benefit derived therefrom.

2. In these cases, the judges and courts shall impose the fine within the limits set for each offense, considering to determine in each case its amount, not only the mitigating and aggravating circumstances of the act, but mainly the economic situation of the guilty party.

3. If, after the sentence, the economic situation of the convicted person worsens, the judge or court, exceptionally and after due investigation of such situation, may reduce the amount of the fine within the limits indicated by law for the offense in question, or authorize its payment in installments to be determined.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

4. In cases in which this Code provides for a fine penalty for legal persons in proportion to the benefit obtained or facilitated, the damage caused, the value of the object, or the amount defrauded or improperly obtained, if calculation based on such concepts is not possible, the Judge or Court shall give reasons for the impossibility of proceeding with such calculation and the fines provided shall be replaced by the following:

- a) Fine of two to five years, if the offense committed by the natural person has a prison sentence of more than five years provided.
- b) Fine of one to three years, if the offense committed by the natural person has a prison sentence of more than two years not included in the preceding paragraph provided.
- c) Fine of six months to two years, in the rest of the cases.

###### Artículo 53.

1. If the convicted person does not satisfy, voluntarily or by enforcement, the fine imposed, they shall be subject to a subsidiary personal liability of one day of deprivation of liberty for every two daily quotas not satisfied, which, in the case of minor offenses, may be served by means of permanent localization. In this case, the limitation on its duration established in paragraph 1 of article 37 shall not apply.

Also the judge or court may, with the consent of the convicted person, agree that the subsidiary liability be served by means of community service. In this case, each day of deprivation of liberty shall be equivalent to one day of work.

2. In cases of proportional fine the Judges and Courts shall establish, according to their prudent discretion, the subsidiary personal liability that applies, which may not exceed, in any case, one year in duration. The Judge or Court may also agree, with the consent of the convicted person, that it be served by means of community service.

3. This subsidiary liability shall not be imposed on those convicted to a custodial sentence exceeding five years.

4. The fulfillment of the subsidiary liability extinguishes the obligation to pay the fine, even if the economic situation of the convicted person improves.

5. Payment of the fine imposed on a legal person may be divided into installments, during a period of up to five years, when its amount demonstrably endangers the survival thereof or the maintenance of existing jobs therein, or when the general interest so advises. If the convicted legal person does not satisfy, voluntarily or by enforcement, the fine imposed within the period that has been set, the Court may agree to its intervention until full payment thereof.

###### Sección 5.ª Of accessory penalties

###### Artículo 54.

Disqualification penalties are accessory in cases in which, without specifically imposing them, the Law declares that other penalties carry them with them.

###### Artículo 55.

A prison sentence equal to or exceeding ten years shall carry with it absolute disqualification for the duration of the sentence, unless it is already provided as a principal penalty for the case in question. The Judge may also order special disqualification for the exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, custody or foster care, or the deprivation of parental authority, when these rights have had a direct relationship with the offense committed. This connection must be expressly determined in the sentence.

###### Artículo 56.

1. In prison sentences of less than ten years, the judges or courts shall impose, having regard to the seriousness of the offense, as accessory penalties, one or more of the following:

1.º Suspension from employment or public office.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

2.º Special disqualification for the right of passive suffrage for the duration of the sentence.

3.º Special disqualification for employment or public office, profession, trade, industry, commerce, exercise of parental authority, guardianship, curatorship, custody or foster care or any other right, the deprivation of parental authority, if these rights have had a direct relationship with the offense committed, this connection having to be expressly determined in the sentence, without prejudice to the application of the provisions of article 579 of this Code.

2. The provisions of this article are understood without prejudice to the application of the provisions of other provisions of this Code regarding the imposition of these penalties.

###### Artículo 57.

1. The judicial authorities, in crimes of homicide, abortion, injuries, against freedom, torture and against moral integrity, trafficking in human beings, against sexual freedom and indemnity, privacy, the right to one's own image and the inviolability of the home, honor, property, the socioeconomic order and family relations, taking into account the seriousness of the acts or the danger represented by the offender, may order in their judgments the imposition of one or more of the prohibitions contemplated in article 48, for a time that shall not exceed ten years if the crime were serious, or five if it were less serious.

Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the convicted person were sentenced to imprisonment and the Judge or Court were to order the imposition of one or more of said prohibitions, it shall do so for a period exceeding by between one and ten years the duration of the prison sentence imposed in the judgment, if the crime were serious, and between one and five years, if it were less serious. In this case, the prison sentence and the aforementioned prohibitions shall necessarily be served by the convicted person simultaneously.

2. In the cases of the crimes mentioned in the first paragraph of section 1 of this article committed against a person who is or has been the spouse, or against a person who is or has been linked to the convicted person by a similar relationship of affection even without cohabitation, or against descendants, ascendants or siblings by nature, adoption or affinity, one's own or those of the spouse or cohabitant, or against minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who live with him or her or who are subject to the authority, guardianship, curatorship, foster care or de facto custody of the spouse or cohabitant, or against a person protected by any other relationship by which he or she is integrated into the core of his or her family cohabitation, as well as against persons who by virtue of their special vulnerability are subject to his or her custody or care in public or private centers, the application of the penalty provided for in section 2 of

###### artículo 48

shall in any case be ordered for a time that shall not exceed ten years if the crime were serious, or five if it were less serious, without prejudice to the provisions of the second paragraph of the preceding section.

3. The prohibitions established in article 48 may also be imposed, for a period of time that shall not exceed six months, for the commission of the crimes mentioned in the first paragraph of section 1 of this article that are considered minor offenses.

###### Sección 6.ª Disposiciones comunes

###### Artículo 58.

1. The time of deprivation of liberty suffered provisionally shall be credited in full by the sentencing Judge or Court for the fulfillment of the sentence or sentences imposed in the case in which said deprivation was ordered, except insofar as it has coincided with any deprivation of liberty imposed on the convicted person in another case, which has been credited or is creditable to him or her in that case. In no case may the same period of deprivation of liberty be credited in more than one case.

2. The crediting of provisional detention in a case other than the one in which it was ordered shall be granted ex officio or at the request of the convicted person and after verification that it has not been credited in another case, by the Penitentiary Supervision Judge of the jurisdiction on which the penitentiary center in which the convicted person is located depends, after hearing the public prosecutor.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

3. The crediting of provisional detention suffered in another case shall only be appropriate when said precautionary measure is subsequent to the criminal acts that motivated the sentence to which it is intended to be credited.

4. The preceding rules shall also apply with respect to deprivations of rights ordered as a precautionary measure.

###### Artículo 59.

When the precautionary measures suffered and the sentence imposed are of a different nature, the Judge or Court shall order that the imposed sentence be considered executed to the extent it deems compensated.

###### Artículo 60.

1. When, after a final judgment has been pronounced, a lasting situation of serious mental disorder is found in the convicted person that prevents him or her from understanding the meaning of the sentence, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge shall suspend the execution of the custodial sentence that has been imposed on him or her, ensuring that he or she receives the necessary medical care, for which purpose he or she may order the imposition of a custodial security measure of those provided for in this Code that may not, in any case, be more onerous than the substituted sentence. If it were a sentence of a different nature, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge shall assess whether the situation of the convicted person allows him or her to understand the meaning of the sentence and, where appropriate, shall suspend the execution by imposing the security measures he or she deems necessary.

The Supervision Judge shall notify the public prosecutor, with sufficient advance notice, of the imminent expiration of the sentence or security measure imposed, for the purposes of the provisions of the first additional provision of this Code.

2. Once the mental health of the convicted person is restored, he or she shall serve the sentence if the sentence has not prescribed, without prejudice to the fact that the Judge or Court, for reasons of equity, may consider the sentence extinguished or reduce its duration, to the extent that the fulfillment of the sentence proves unnecessary or counterproductive.

##### CAPÍTULO II

De la aplicación de las penas

###### Sección 1.ª Reglas generales para la aplicación de las penas

###### Artículo 61.

When the Law establishes a sentence, it is understood to impose it on the perpetrators of the consummated offense.

###### Artículo 62.

The perpetrators of an attempted crime shall be imposed the sentence one or two degrees lower than that indicated by Law for the consummated crime, to the extent deemed appropriate, taking into account the danger inherent in the attempt and the degree of execution achieved.

###### Artículo 63.

Accomplices to a consummated or attempted crime shall be imposed the sentence one degree lower than that established by Law for the perpetrators of the same crime.

###### Artículo 64.

The preceding rules shall not apply in cases where attempt and complicity are specially punished by Law.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Artículo 65.

1. Aggravating or mitigating circumstances that consist of any cause of a personal nature shall aggravate or mitigate the liability only of those in whom they occur.

2. Those that consist of the material execution of the act or the means employed to carry it out, shall serve only to aggravate or mitigate the liability of those who have had knowledge of them at the time of the action or of their cooperation in the crime.

3. When the conditions, qualities or personal relationships that ground the culpability of the perpetrator are not present in the inducer or necessary cooperator, the judges or courts may impose the sentence one degree lower than that indicated by law for the offense in question.

###### Article 66.

1. In the application of the penalty, in the case of intentional offences, the judges or courts shall observe, depending on whether or not there are mitigating or aggravating circumstances, the following rules: 1st When only one mitigating circumstance is present, they shall apply the penalty in the lower half of that established by law for the offence.

2nd When two or more mitigating circumstances are present, or one or more highly qualified ones, and no aggravating circumstance is present, they shall apply the penalty one or two degrees lower than that established by law, taking into account the number and nature of said mitigating circumstances.

3rd When only one or two aggravating circumstances are present, they shall apply the penalty in the upper half of that established by law for the offence.

4th When more than two aggravating circumstances are present and no mitigating circumstance is present, they may apply the penalty one degree higher than that established by law, in its lower half.

5th When the aggravating circumstance of recidivism is present with the qualification that the guilty party, when offending, had been finally convicted of at least three offences included in the same title of this Code, provided they are of the same nature, they may apply the penalty one degree higher than that provided by law for the offence in question, taking into account previous convictions, as well as the seriousness of the new offence committed.

For the purposes of this rule, criminal records that have been cancelled or should have been cancelled shall not be counted.

6th When neither mitigating nor aggravating circumstances are present, they shall apply the penalty established by law for the offence committed, to the extent they deem appropriate, taking into account the personal circumstances of the offender and the greater or lesser seriousness of the act. 7th When mitigating and aggravating circumstances are present, they shall assess and rationally offset them for the individualization of the penalty. In the event that a qualified ground for mitigation persists, they shall apply the penalty one degree lower. If a qualified ground for aggravation remains, they shall apply the penalty in its upper half.

8th When the judges or courts apply the penalty lower by more than one degree, they may do so to its full extent.

2. In minor offences and in negligent offences, the judges or courts shall apply the penalties at their prudent discretion, without being subject to the rules prescribed in the preceding paragraph.

###### Article 66 bis.

In the application of penalties imposed on legal persons, the provisions of rules 1st to 4th and 6th to 8th of the first paragraph of Article 66 shall apply, as well as the following: 1st In cases where they are established by the provisions of Book II, to decide on the imposition and extent of the penalties provided for in letters b) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33, the following must be taken into account:

- a) Their necessity to prevent the continuity of the criminal activity or its effects.
- b) Their economic and social consequences, and especially the effects on workers.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

c) The position within the structure of the legal person occupied by the natural person or body that failed to fulfil the duty of control.

2nd When the penalties provided for in letters c) to g) of paragraph 7 of Article 33 are imposed with a limited duration, this may not exceed the maximum duration of the custodial sentence provided for the case where the offence was committed by a natural person. For the imposition of the sanctions provided for in letters c) to g) for a period exceeding two years, it shall be necessary that one of the following two circumstances be present:

- a) That the legal person is a recidivist.
- b) That the legal person is used instrumentally for the commission of criminal offences. It shall be understood that this last case is present whenever the legal activity of the legal person is less relevant than its illegal activity.

When the liability of the legal person, in the cases provided for in letter b) of paragraph 1 of Article 31 bis, derives from a failure to fulfil the duties of supervision, oversight and control that is not of a serious nature, these penalties shall in any case have a maximum duration of two years.

For the imposition on a permanent basis of the sanctions provided for in letters b) and e), and for the imposition for a period exceeding five years of those provided for in letters e) and

f) of paragraph 7 of Article 33, it shall be necessary that one of the following two circumstances be present:

- a) That the factual situation provided for in rule 5th of paragraph 1 of

###### Article 66 be present.

b) That the legal person is used instrumentally for the commission of criminal offences. It shall be understood that this last case is present whenever the legal activity of the legal person is less relevant than its illegal activity.

###### Article 67.

The rules of the preceding article shall not apply to aggravating or mitigating circumstances that the Law has taken into account when describing or sanctioning an infringement, nor to those that are so inherent to the offence that without their presence it could not be committed.

###### Article 68.

In the cases provided for in the first circumstance of Article 21, the judges or courts shall impose the penalty one or two degrees lower than that indicated by law, taking into account the number and nature of the requirements that are absent or present, and the personal circumstances of its perpetrator, without prejudice to the application of Article 66 of this Code.

###### Article 69.

To a person over eighteen years of age and under twenty-one who commits a criminal act, the provisions of the law regulating the criminal liability of minors may be applied in the cases and with the requirements that it provides.

###### Artículo 70.

1. The penalty one degree higher or lower than that provided by law for any offense shall have the extent resulting from the application of the following rules:

1.ª The penalty one degree higher shall be formed starting from the maximum figure set by law for the offense in question and increasing it by half of its amount, with the resulting sum constituting its maximum limit. The minimum limit of the penalty one degree higher shall be the maximum of the penalty set by law for the offense in question, increased by one day or one day-fine according to the nature of the penalty to be imposed.

2.ª The penalty one degree lower shall be formed starting from the minimum figure set for the offense in question and deducting from it half of its amount, with the result of such deduction constituting its minimum limit. The maximum limit of the penalty one degree lower shall be the

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

minimum of the penalty set by law for the offense in question, reduced by one day or one day-fine according to the nature of the penalty to be imposed.

2. For the purposes of determining the upper or lower half of the penalty or of specifying the penalty one degree lower or higher, the day or day-fine shall be considered indivisible and shall act as penological units of more or less, as the case may be.
