3. When, in the application of rule 1.ª of paragraph 1 of this article, the penalty one degree higher exceeds the maximum limits established for each penalty in this Code, the following shall be considered immediately higher:

1.º If the penalty determined were imprisonment, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 30 years.

2.º If it were absolute or special disqualification, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 30 years.

3.º If it were suspension from employment or public office, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be eight years.

4.º In the case of deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 15 years.

5.º In the case of deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.

6.º In the case of deprivation of the right to reside in certain places or go to them, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.

7.º In the case of prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.

8.º In the case of prohibition from communicating with the victim or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 20 years.

9.º If it were a fine, the same penalty, with the clause that its maximum duration shall be 30 months.

4. The penalty one degree lower than permanent imprisonment is the penalty of imprisonment of twenty to thirty years.

###### Artículo 71.

1. In determining the penalty one degree lower, judges or courts shall not be limited by the minimum amounts set by law for each type of penalty, but may reduce them in the manner resulting from the application of the corresponding rule.

2. Notwithstanding, when by application of the previous rules it is appropriate to impose a prison sentence of less than three months, this shall in all cases be replaced by a fine, community service, or permanent location monitoring, even though the law does not provide for these penalties for the offense in question, substituting each day of imprisonment with two fine units or one day of work or one day of permanent location monitoring.

###### Artículo 72.

Judges or courts, in the application of the penalty, in accordance with the rules contained in this chapter, shall provide reasoning in the judgment for the degree and specific extent of that imposed.

###### Sección 2.ª Reglas especiales para la aplicación de las penas

###### Artículo 73.

The person responsible for two or more offenses shall be imposed all the penalties corresponding to the various infractions for their simultaneous service, if possible, by the nature and effects thereof.

###### Artículo 74.

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the previous article, whoever, in execution of a preconceived plan or taking advantage of an identical occasion, carries out a plurality of actions or

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

omissions that offend one or several subjects and violate the same criminal provision or provisions of the same or similar nature, shall be punished as the author of a continued offense with the penalty prescribed for the most serious infraction, which shall be imposed in its upper half, and may reach up to the lower half of the penalty one degree higher.

2. If they were offenses against property, the penalty shall be imposed taking into account the total damage caused. In these infractions the Judge or Court shall impose, with reasoning, the penalty one or two degrees higher, to the extent it deems appropriate, if the act were of notorious gravity and had harmed a generality of persons.

3. Excluded from the provisions of the previous paragraphs are offenses against eminently personal interests, except those constituting infractions against honor and sexual freedom and indemnity that affect the same victim. In these cases, the nature of the act and the provision violated shall be considered in order to apply or not apply continued offense.

###### Artículo 75.

When all or some of the penalties corresponding to the various infractions cannot be served simultaneously by the convicted person, the order of their respective gravity shall be followed for their successive service, insofar as possible.

###### Artículo 76.

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the previous article, the maximum effective service of the sentence of the guilty party may not exceed triple the time for which the most serious of the penalties they have incurred is imposed, declaring extinguished those that proceed once those already imposed cover said maximum, which may not exceed 20 years. Exceptionally, this maximum limit shall be:

- a) 25 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offenses and one of them is punishable by law with a prison sentence of up to 20 years.
- b) 30 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offenses and one of them is punishable by law with a prison sentence exceeding 20 years.
- c) 40 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offenses and at least two of them are punishable by law with a prison sentence exceeding 20 years.
- d) 40 years, when the subject has been convicted of two or more offenses relating to terrorist organizations and groups and terrorism offenses under Chapter VII of Title XXII of

## Libro II

of this Code and one of them is punishable by law with a prison sentence exceeding 20 years.

e) When the subject has been convicted of two or more offenses and at least one of them is punishable by law with a reviewable permanent prison sentence, the provisions of articles 92 and 78 bis shall apply.

2. The limitation shall apply even when the penalties have been imposed in different proceedings when they have been imposed for acts committed before the date on which those that, being the object of accumulation, had been tried first were prosecuted.

###### Artículo 77.

1. The provisions of the two preceding articles are not applicable in the case where a single act constitutes two or more offenses, or when one of them is a necessary means to commit the other.

2. In the first case, the penalty provided for the most serious offense shall be applied in its upper half, without exceeding that which represents the sum of those that would correspond to apply if the offenses were punished separately. When the penalty thus computed exceeds this limit, the offenses shall be punished separately.

3. In the second, a penalty higher than that which would have corresponded, in the specific case, for the most serious offense shall be imposed, and which may not exceed the sum of the specific penalties that would have been imposed separately for each of the offenses. Within these limits, the judge or court shall individualize the penalty in accordance with the criteria expressed

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

in article 66. In any case, the penalty imposed may not exceed the duration limit provided in the preceding article.

###### Artículo 78.

1. If as a consequence of the limitations established in paragraph 1 of article 76 the penalty to be served were to be less than half of the total sum of those imposed, the sentencing judge or court may agree that the penitentiary benefits, the exit permits, the classification in third grade and the computation of time for conditional release refer to the totality of the penalties imposed in the sentences.

2. In these cases, the supervisory judge, following an individualized and favorable prognosis of social reintegration and assessing, where appropriate, the personal circumstances of the offender and the evolution of the re-educational treatment, may reasonably agree, having heard the Public Prosecutor's Office, Penitentiary Institutions and the other parties, the application of the general regime of compliance.

If it concerns offenses relating to terrorist organizations and groups and terrorism offenses of Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, or committed within criminal organizations, and taking into account the total sum of the penalties imposed, the foregoing possibility shall only be applicable:

- a) To the third penitentiary grade, when one-fifth of the maximum limit of compliance of the sentence remains to be served.
- b) To conditional release, when one-eighth of the maximum limit of compliance of the sentence remains to be served.

###### Artículo 78 bis.

1. When the subject has been convicted of two or more offenses and, at least, one of them is punished by law with reviewable permanent imprisonment, the progression to third grade shall require compliance with:

- a) a minimum of eighteen years of imprisonment, when the convicted person has been so for several offenses, one of them is punished with reviewable permanent imprisonment and the rest of the penalties imposed total more than five years.
- b) a minimum of twenty years of imprisonment, when the convicted person has been so for several offenses, one of them is punished with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment and the rest of the penalties imposed total more than fifteen years.
- c) a minimum of twenty-two years of imprisonment, when the convicted person has been so for several offenses and two or more of them are punished with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment, or one of them is punished with a penalty of reviewable permanent imprisonment and the rest of penalties imposed total twenty-five years or more.
  - 2. In these cases, the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the penalty shall require that the convicted person has served:
- a) A minimum of twenty-five years of imprisonment, in the cases referred to in letters a) and b) of the preceding paragraph.
- b) A minimum of thirty years of imprisonment in that of letter c) of the preceding paragraph.
  - 3. If it concerns offenses relating to terrorist organizations and groups and terrorism offenses of Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, or committed within criminal organizations, the minimum compliance limits for access to the third grade of classification shall be twenty-four years of imprisonment, in the cases referred to in letters a) and b) of the first paragraph, and thirty-two years of imprisonment in that of letter c) of the first paragraph.

In these cases, the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the penalty shall require that the convicted person has served a minimum of twenty-eight years of imprisonment, in the cases referred to in letters a) and b) of the first paragraph, and thirty-five years of imprisonment in that of letter b)(*) of the first paragraph.

(*) We understand that it refers to letter c) of the first paragraph.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Artículo 79.

Whenever the Judges or Courts impose a penalty that carries with it other accessory penalties, they shall also expressly sentence the offender to the latter.

##### CAPÍTULO III

On the substitute forms of execution of custodial sentences and conditional release.

###### Section 1 On the suspension of execution of custodial sentences

###### Article 80.

1. Judges or courts, by means of a reasoned decision, may suspend the execution of custodial sentences not exceeding two years when it is reasonable to expect that the execution of the sentence will not be necessary to prevent the convicted person from committing new offenses in the future.

To adopt this decision, the judge or court shall assess the circumstances of the offense committed, the personal circumstances of the convicted person, their record, their conduct after the act, in particular their effort to repair the damage caused, their family and social circumstances, and the effects that may be expected from the suspension of execution itself and from compliance with the measures that may be imposed.

2. The following shall be necessary conditions for suspending the execution of the sentence:

1.ª That the convicted person has offended for the first time. For this purpose, previous convictions for negligent offenses or minor offenses shall not be taken into account, nor shall criminal records that have been canceled, or should be canceled in accordance with the provisions of article 136. Neither shall criminal records corresponding to offenses that, by their nature or circumstances, lack relevance for assessing the probability of committing future offenses be taken into account.

2.ª That the sentence or the sum of those imposed does not exceed two years, not including in such calculation that derived from non-payment of the fine.

3.ª That the civil liabilities that may have arisen have been satisfied and that the confiscation ordered in the judgment pursuant to article 127 has been made effective.

This requirement shall be deemed fulfilled when the convicted person assumes the commitment to satisfy the civil liabilities according to their economic capacity and to facilitate the ordered confiscation, and it is reasonable to expect that it will be fulfilled within the prudent period that the judge or court determines. The judge or court, in view of the scope of the civil liability and the social impact of the offense, may request the guarantees it deems appropriate to ensure its fulfillment.

3. Exceptionally, even if conditions 1.ª and 2.ª of the preceding paragraph do not concur, and provided that they are not habitual offenders, the suspension of prison sentences that individually do not exceed two years may be agreed when the personal circumstances of the offender, the nature of the act, their conduct and, in particular, the effort to repair the damage caused, so advise.

In these cases, the suspension shall always be conditioned on the effective reparation of the damage or compensation for the harm caused according to their physical and economic possibilities, or on compliance with the agreement referred to in measure 1.ª of article 84. Likewise, one of the measures referred to in numerals 2.ª or 3.ª of the same provision shall always be imposed, with an extension that may not be less than that resulting from applying the conversion criteria set forth therein on one-fifth of the imposed sentence.

4. Judges and courts may grant the suspension of any imposed sentence without being subject to any requirement in the event that the convicted person suffers from a very serious illness with incurable suffering, unless at the time of commission of the offense they already had another sentence suspended for the same reason.

5. Even when conditions 1.ª and 2.ª provided in paragraph 2 of this article do not concur, the judge or court may agree to suspend the execution of custodial sentences

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

not exceeding five years for convicted persons who have committed the criminal act because of their dependence on the substances indicated in numeral 2 of

###### article 20, provided that it is sufficiently certified, by a duly accredited or approved public or private center or service,

that the convicted person is rehabilitated or undergoing treatment for that purpose at the time of deciding on the suspension. The judge or court may order the necessary verifications to be carried out to verify compliance with the above requirements.

In the event that the convicted person is undergoing rehabilitation treatment, the suspension of execution of the sentence shall also be conditioned on their not abandoning the treatment until its completion. Relapses in treatment shall not be deemed abandonment if they do not evidence a definitive abandonment of the rehabilitation treatment.

6. In offenses that can only be prosecuted upon complaint or accusation by the offended party, judges and courts shall hear the latter and, where applicable, their representative, before granting the benefits of suspension of execution of the sentence.

###### Article 81.

The suspension period shall be from two to five years for custodial sentences not exceeding two years, and from three months to one year for minor sentences, and shall be fixed by the judge or court, taking into account the criteria expressed in the second paragraph of paragraph 1 of

###### article 80.

In the event that the suspension has been granted in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 5 of the preceding article, the suspension period shall be from three to five years.

###### Article 82.

1. The judge or court shall decide in the judgment on the suspension of execution of the sentence whenever possible. In other cases, once the judgment has become final, it shall rule with the utmost urgency, after hearing the parties, on the granting or not of the suspension of execution of the sentence.

2. The suspension period shall be computed from the date of the decision granting it. If the suspension has been granted in the judgment, the suspension period shall be computed from the date on which it became final.

The period during which the convicted person remained in a situation of absconding shall not be counted as suspension period.

###### Article 83.

1. The judge or court may condition the suspension on compliance with the following prohibitions and duties when this is necessary to avoid the danger of committing new offenses, without imposing duties and obligations that are excessive and disproportionate:

1.ª Prohibition from approaching the victim or those of their family members or other persons determined by the judge or court, their homes, their workplaces or other places habitually frequented by them, or from communicating with them by any means. The imposition of this prohibition shall always be communicated to the persons in relation to whom it is agreed.

2.ª Prohibition from establishing contact with specific persons or with members of a specific group, when there are indications that allow to reasonably suppose that such subjects may facilitate the opportunity to commit new offenses or incite them to do so.

3.ª Maintain their place of residence in a specific place with prohibition from leaving it or being temporarily absent without authorization from the judge or court.

4.ª Prohibition from residing in a specific place or going to it, when in them they may find the opportunity or motive to commit new offenses.

5.ª Appear personally with the periodicity determined before the judge or court, police stations or administration service determined, to report on their activities and justify them.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

6.ª Participate in educational, employment, cultural, road safety, sexual education, environmental protection, animal protection, equal treatment and non-discrimination, peaceful conflict resolution, positive parenting and other similar programs.

7.ª Participate in programs for withdrawal from consumption of alcohol, toxic drugs or narcotic substances, or for treatment of other addictive behaviors.

8.ª Prohibition from driving motor vehicles that do not have technological devices that condition their ignition or operation to prior verification of the driver's physical conditions, when the subject has been convicted of an offense against road safety and the measure is necessary to prevent the possible commission of new offenses.

9.ª Fulfill the other duties that the judge or court deems appropriate for the social rehabilitation of the convicted person, with their prior consent, provided they do not violate their dignity as a person.

2. When it concerns offenses committed against a woman by whoever is or has been her spouse, or by whoever is or has been linked to her by a similar relationship of affection, even without cohabitation, the prohibitions and duties indicated in rules 1.ª, 4.ª and 6.ª of the preceding section shall always be imposed.

The foregoing prohibitions and duties shall also be imposed when it concerns offenses against sexual freedom, forced marriage, female genital mutilation and human trafficking.

3. The imposition of any of the prohibitions or duties of rules 1.ª, 2.ª, 3.ª, or 4.ª of section 1 of this article shall be communicated to the State Security Forces and Corps, which shall ensure their compliance. Any possible breach or relevant circumstance to assess the dangerousness of the convicted person and the possibility of future commission of new offenses shall be immediately communicated to the Public Prosecutor's Office and to the enforcement judge or court.

4. The monitoring of compliance with the duties referred to in rules 6.ª, 7.ª and 8.ª of section 1 of this article shall correspond to the prison Administration's services for management of sentences and alternative measures. These services shall inform the enforcement judge or court on compliance with a periodicity of at least quarterly, in the case of rules 6.ª and 8.ª, and biannually, in the case of 7.ª and, in any event, upon its conclusion. Likewise, they shall immediately report any relevant circumstance to assess the dangerousness of the convicted person and the possibility of future commission of new offenses, as well as breaches of the imposed obligation or its effective compliance.

###### Article 84.

1. The judge or court may also condition the suspension of the execution of the sentence on compliance with any one or more of the following benefits or measures:

1.ª Compliance with the agreement reached by the parties by virtue of mediation.

2.ª Payment of a fine, the extent of which shall be determined by the judge or court in view of the circumstances of the case, which may not exceed that which would result from applying two fine units for each day of imprisonment over a maximum limit of two thirds of its duration.

3.ª Performance of community service work, especially when appropriate as a form of symbolic reparation in view of the circumstances of the act and the perpetrator. The duration of this work service shall be determined by the judge or court in view of the circumstances of the case, without exceeding that which results from computing one day of work for each day of imprisonment over a maximum limit of two thirds of its duration.

2. If it concerned an offense committed against a woman by whoever is or has been her spouse, or by whoever is or has been linked to her by a similar relationship of affection, even without cohabitation, or against descendants, ascendants or siblings by nature, adoption or affinity of their own or of the spouse or cohabiting partner, or against minors or persons with disabilities in need of special protection who live with them or who are subject to the authority, guardianship, curatorship, foster care or de facto custody of the spouse or cohabiting partner, payment of the fine referred to in measure 2.ª of the preceding section

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

may only be imposed when it is proven that between them there are no economic relations derived from a marital relationship, cohabitation or filiation, or from the existence of common descendants.

###### Article 85.

During the period of suspension of the sentence, and in view of the possible modification of the circumstances assessed, the judge or court may modify the decision previously adopted in accordance with Articles 83 and 84, and agree to the lifting of all or any of the prohibitions, duties or benefits that have been agreed upon, their modification or substitution by others that are less burdensome.

###### Article 86.

1. The judge or court shall revoke the suspension and order the execution of the sentence when the convicted person:

- a) Is convicted of a crime committed during the suspension period and this reveals that the expectation on which the suspension decision was based can no longer be maintained.
- b) Seriously or repeatedly breaches the prohibitions and duties that have been imposed pursuant to Article 83, or evades the control of the sentence management and alternative measures services of the Prison Administration.
- c) Seriously or repeatedly breaches the conditions that have been imposed for the suspension pursuant to Article 84.
- d) Provides inaccurate or insufficient information about the whereabouts of property or objects whose confiscation has been ordered; fails to comply with the commitment to pay the civil liabilities to which they have been sentenced, unless they lack the financial capacity to do so; or provides inaccurate or insufficient information about their assets, breaching the obligation imposed in Article 589 of the Civil Procedure Act.
  - 2. If the breach of the prohibitions, duties or conditions has not been serious or repeated, the judge or court may:
- a) Impose new prohibitions, duties or conditions on the convicted person, or modify those already imposed.
- b) Extend the suspension period, which in no case may exceed half the duration of the period that was initially set.
  - 3. In the case of revocation of the suspension, the expenses incurred by the convicted person to repair the damage caused by the crime pursuant to paragraph 1 of Article 84 shall not be restituted. However, the judge or court shall credit against the sentence the payments and provision of work that have been made or fulfilled pursuant to measures 2 and 3.
  - 4. In all the above cases, the judge or court shall decide after having heard the Prosecutor and the other parties. However, they may revoke the suspension of the execution of the sentence and order the immediate imprisonment of the convicted person when it is essential to avoid the risk of criminal recidivism, the risk of the convicted person fleeing, or to ensure the protection of the victim.

The judge or court may order the performance of such verification procedures as may be necessary and order the holding of an oral hearing when deemed necessary to reach a decision.

###### Article 87.

1. Once the set suspension period has elapsed without the subject having committed a crime that reveals that the expectation on which the suspension decision was based can no longer be maintained, and the rules of conduct set by the judge or court having been sufficiently complied with, the latter shall order the remission of the sentence.

2. However, to order the remission of the sentence that has been suspended pursuant to paragraph 5 of Article 80, proof must be provided of the subject's rehabilitation or the continuity of treatment. Otherwise, the judge or court shall order its enforcement,

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

unless, having heard the relevant reports, they deem the continuation of treatment necessary; in such case they may grant, with reasoning, an extension of the suspension period for a period not exceeding two years.

###### Section 2.ª On the substitution of custodial sentences

###### Article 88.

(Repealed).

###### Article 89.

1. Prison sentences of more than one year imposed on a foreign citizen shall be replaced by their expulsion from Spanish territory. Exceptionally, when necessary to ensure the defence of the legal order and restore confidence in the validity of the rule violated by the crime, the judge or court may agree to the execution of a part of the sentence that may not exceed two thirds of its length, and the replacement of the remainder by the expulsion of the convicted person from Spanish territory. In any case, the remainder of the sentence shall be replaced by the expulsion of the convicted person from Spanish territory when he or she is granted third grade or conditional release.

2. When a sentence of more than five years of imprisonment has been imposed, or several sentences exceeding that duration, the judge or court shall agree to the execution of all or part of the sentence, to the extent necessary to ensure the defence of the legal order and restore confidence in the validity of the rule violated by the crime. In these cases, the execution of the remainder of the sentence shall be replaced by the expulsion of the convicted person from Spanish territory when the convicted person serves the part of the sentence that has been determined, is granted third grade or is granted conditional release.

3. The judge or court shall rule in the judgment on the replacement of the execution of the sentence whenever possible. In other cases, once the judgment becomes final, the court shall rule with the utmost urgency, after hearing the Public Prosecutor and the other parties, on whether or not to grant the replacement of the execution of the sentence.

4. Replacement shall not be appropriate when, in view of the circumstances of the act and the personal circumstances of the perpetrator, particularly his or her roots in Spain, the expulsion would be disproportionate.

The expulsion of a citizen of the European Union shall only be appropriate when it represents a serious threat to public order or public safety in view of the nature, circumstances and seriousness of the crime committed, his or her criminal record and personal circumstances.

If he or she has resided in Spain for the previous ten years, expulsion shall be appropriate when, in addition:

- a) He or she has been convicted of one or more crimes against life, liberty, physical integrity and sexual freedom and indemnity punishable by a maximum prison sentence of more than five years and there is a well-founded assessment of a serious risk that he or she may commit crimes of the same nature.
- b) He or she has been convicted of one or more terrorism offences or other crimes committed within a criminal group or organisation.

In these cases, the provisions of paragraph 2 of this article shall apply in any case.

5. The foreigner may not return to Spain for a period of five to ten years, counted from the date of his or her expulsion, taking into account the duration of the replaced sentence and the personal circumstances of the convicted person.

6. The expulsion shall entail the closure of any administrative procedure that had as its object the authorisation to reside or work in Spain.

7. If the expelled foreigner returns to Spain before the judicially established period of time has elapsed, he or she shall serve the sentences that were replaced, unless, exceptionally, the judge or court reduces their duration when their service is unnecessary to ensure the defence of the legal order and restore confidence in the

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

legal rule violated by the crime, taking into account the time elapsed since the expulsion and the circumstances in which its breach has occurred.

However, if caught at the border, he or she shall be expelled directly by the government authority, and the prohibition period shall begin to run again in its entirety.

8. When, upon agreeing to the expulsion in any of the cases provided for in this article, the foreigner is not or does not remain effectively deprived of liberty in execution of the imposed sentence, the judge or court may agree, in order to ensure the expulsion, his or her admission to a detention centre for foreigners, under the terms and with the limits and guarantees provided for in the law for government expulsion.

In any case, if after agreeing to the replacement of the custodial sentence by expulsion, the latter cannot be carried out, the originally imposed sentence or the pending period of conviction shall be executed, or the suspension of its execution shall be applied, as the case may be.

9. Sentences imposed for the commission of the crimes referred to in articles 177 bis, 312, 313 and 318 bis shall not be replaced.

###### Section 3 On Parole

###### Article 90.

1. The prison supervisory judge shall order the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the prison sentence and grant parole to the convicted person who meets the following requirements:

- a) That they are classified in the third grade.
- b) That they have served three-quarters of the imposed sentence.
- c) That they have shown good conduct.

To rule on the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and granting of parole, the prison supervisory judge shall assess the personality of the convicted person, their record, the circumstances of the offence committed, the importance of the legal interests that could be affected by a repetition of the offence, their conduct during the serving of the sentence, their family and social circumstances and the effects that may be expected from the suspension of the execution itself and from compliance with the measures that may be imposed.

The suspension shall not be granted if the convicted person has not satisfied the civil liability arising from the offence in the cases and in accordance with the criteria established by paragraphs 5 and 6 of article 72 of Organic Law 1/1979, of 26 September, General Penitentiary.

2. The suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence may also be ordered and parole granted to convicted persons who meet the following requirements:

- a) That they have served two-thirds of their sentence.
- b) That during the serving of their sentence they have carried out work, cultural or occupational activities, either continuously, or with such benefit that has resulted in a relevant and favourable modification of those of their personal circumstances related to their prior criminal activity.
- c) That they prove compliance with the requirements referred to in the previous paragraph, except that of having served three-quarters of their sentence.

At the proposal of the Penitentiary Institutions and following a report from the Public Prosecutor's Office and the other parties, once the circumstances of letters a) and c) of the previous paragraph have been met, the prison supervisory judge may bring forward, once half of the sentence has been served, the granting of parole in relation to the period provided for in the previous paragraph, up to a maximum of ninety days for each year elapsed of actual serving of the sentence. This measure shall require that the convicted person has continuously carried out the activities indicated in letter b) of this paragraph and that they also prove effective and favourable participation in victim reparation programmes or treatment or detoxification programmes, as applicable.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

3. Exceptionally, the prison supervisory judge may order the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and grant parole to convicted persons in whom the following requirements are met:

- a) That they are serving their first prison sentence and that it does not exceed three years in duration.
- b) That they have served half of their sentence.
- c) That they prove compliance with the requirements referred to in paragraph 1, except that of having served three-quarters of their sentence, as well as that regulated in letter b) of the previous paragraph.

This regime shall not be applicable to those who have been convicted for the commission of an offence against sexual freedom and integrity.

4. The prison supervisory judge may deny the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence when the convicted person has given inaccurate or insufficient information about the whereabouts of goods or objects whose confiscation has been ordered; fails to comply according to their capacity with the commitment to pay the civil liabilities to which they have been sentenced; or provides inaccurate or insufficient information about their assets, breaching the obligation imposed in article 589 of the Civil Procedure Act. They may also deny the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence imposed for any of the offences provided for in Title XIX of Book II of this Code, when the convicted person has evaded compliance with the pecuniary liabilities or the reparation of the economic damage caused to the Administration to which they have been sentenced.

5. In cases of suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and granting of parole, the rules contained in articles 83, 86 and 87 shall be applicable. The prison supervisory judge, in view of the possible modification of the assessed circumstances, may modify the decision previously adopted in accordance with article 83, and order the imposition of new prohibitions, duties or obligations, the modification of those already agreed or the lifting thereof.

Likewise, the prison supervisory judge shall revoke the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the parole granted when a change in the circumstances that gave rise to the suspension becomes apparent that no longer allows maintaining the prognosis of lack of dangerousness on which the decision adopted was based. The period of suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence shall be from two to five years. In any case, the period of suspension of execution and parole may not be less than the duration of the part of the sentence pending compliance. The period of suspension and parole shall be counted from the date of release of the convicted person.

6. The revocation of the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and parole shall give rise to the execution of the part of the sentence pending compliance. The time elapsed on parole shall not be counted as time of serving the sentence.

7. The prison supervisory judge shall rule ex officio on the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and granting of parole at the request of the convicted person. In the event that the request is not granted, the judge or court may set a period of six months, which may be extended with justification to one year, before the claim may be raised again.

8. In the case of persons convicted of offences committed within criminal organisations or of any of the offences regulated in Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence imposed and granting of parole requires that the convicted person show unequivocal signs of having abandoned the aims and means of terrorist activity and has actively collaborated with the authorities, either to prevent the commission of other offences by the terrorist organisation or group, or to mitigate the effects of their offence, or for the identification, capture and prosecution of those responsible for terrorist offences, to obtain evidence or to prevent the action or development of the organisations or associations to which they have belonged or with which they have collaborated, which may be proven by an express statement repudiating their criminal activities and abandoning violence and an express request for forgiveness from the victims of their offence, as well as by the

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

technical reports that prove that the prisoner is truly disassociated from the terrorist organisation and from the environment and activities of illegal associations and collectives surrounding it and their collaboration with the authorities.

Paragraphs 2 and 3 shall not be applicable to persons convicted of the commission of any of the offences regulated in Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code or of offences committed within criminal organisations.

###### Article 91.

1. Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding article, convicted persons who have reached the age of seventy years, or reach it during the serving of the sentence, and meet the requirements demanded in the preceding article, except that of having served three-quarters thereof, two-thirds or, as the case may be, half of the sentence, may obtain the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the granting of conditional release.

The same criterion shall apply when it concerns very seriously ill persons with incurable ailments, and this is so established following the practice of the medical reports that, at the discretion of the prison supervision judge, are deemed necessary.

2. When it is established by the prison administration that the inmate is in any of the cases provided for in the preceding paragraphs, it shall submit the conditional release file, with the urgency that the case requires, to the prison supervision judge, who, at the time of deciding it, shall assess together with the personal circumstances the difficulty to commit offenses and the low dangerousness of the subject.

3. If the danger to the life of the inmate, due to his illness or advanced age, is manifest, as evidenced by the opinion of the forensic physician and of the medical services of the prison establishment, the judge or court may, without the need to prove compliance with any other requirement and having assessed the lack of relevant dangerousness of the convicted person, order the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and grant him conditional release with no other formality than requiring from the prison center the final prognosis report in order to be able to make the assessment referred to in the preceding paragraph.

In this case, the convicted person shall be obliged to provide the prison medical service, the forensic physician, or any other person determined by the judge or court, with the necessary information to be able to assess the evolution of his illness.

Failure to comply with this obligation may give rise to the revocation of the suspension of execution and of conditional release.

4. The provisions contained in paragraphs 4, 5 and 6 of the preceding article are applicable to the case regulated in this article.

###### Article 92.

1. The court shall order the suspension of the execution of the reviewable permanent prison sentence when the following requirements are met:

- a) That the convicted person has served twenty-five years of his sentence, without prejudice to the provisions of article 78 bis for the cases regulated therein.
- b) That he is classified in third grade.
- c) That the court, in view of the personality of the convicted person, his background, the circumstances of the offense committed, the relevance of the legal interests that could be affected by a repetition of the offense, his conduct during the serving of the sentence, his family and social circumstances, and the effects that may be expected from the suspension of execution itself and from compliance with the measures that may be imposed, can establish, following assessment of the progress reports submitted by the prison center and by those specialists that the court itself determines, the existence of a favorable prognosis for social reintegration.

In the case that the convicted person has been convicted for several offenses, the examination of the requirements referred to in letter c) shall be carried out by assessing all the offenses committed as a whole.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

The court shall rule on the suspension of the reviewable permanent prison sentence after an adversarial oral proceeding in which the Public Prosecutor's Office and the convicted person, assisted by his lawyer, shall participate.

2. If it concerns offenses relating to terrorist organizations and groups and offenses of terrorism of Chapter VII of Title XXII of Book II of this Code, it shall also be necessary that the convicted person shows unequivocal signs of having abandoned the aims and means of terrorist activity and has actively collaborated with the authorities, either to prevent the commission of other offenses by the terrorist organization or group, or to mitigate the effects of his offense, or for the identification, capture and prosecution of those responsible for terrorist offenses, to obtain evidence or to prevent the action or development of the organizations or associations to which he has belonged or with which he has collaborated, which may be proven by means of an express declaration of repudiation of his criminal activities and abandonment of violence and an express request for forgiveness from the victims of his offense, as well as by technical reports that prove that the prisoner is truly dissociated from the terrorist organization and from the environment and activities of illegal associations and collectives that surround it and his collaboration with the authorities.

3. The suspension of execution shall have a duration of five to ten years. The period of suspension and conditional release shall be computed from the date of release of the convicted person. The rules contained in the second paragraph of paragraph 1 of

###### article 80 and in articles 83, 86, 87 and 91 are applicable.

The judge or court, in view of the possible modification of the assessed circumstances, may modify the decision previously adopted pursuant to article 83, and order the imposition of new prohibitions, duties or obligations, the modification of those already agreed upon, or the lifting thereof.

Likewise, the prison supervision judge shall revoke the suspension of the execution of the remainder of the sentence and the conditional release granted when a change in the circumstances that gave rise to the suspension becomes manifest that no longer allows maintaining the prognosis of lack of dangerousness on which the decision adopted was based.

4. Once the part of the sentence referred to in letter a) of paragraph 1 of this article or, as the case may be, in article 78 bis, has been served, the court must verify, at least every two years, compliance with the remaining requirements of conditional release. The court shall also rule on requests for the granting of conditional release of the convicted person, but may set a period of up to one year within which, after a request has been rejected, no action shall be taken on his new applications.

Please note that paragraph 3, third subparagraph, and paragraph 4, in the wording given by sole art. 51 of Organic Law 1/2015, of 30 March, are not unconstitutional provided that they are interpreted in the sense established in legal ground 9 b) of Constitutional Court Judgment 169/2021, of 6 October. Ref. BOE-A-2021-18372

###### Article 93.

(Deleted).

###### Section 4.ª Common Provisions

###### Article 94.

For the purposes provided for in section 2.ª of this chapter, habitual offenders are considered to be those who have committed three or more offenses included in the same chapter, within a period not exceeding five years, and have been convicted thereof.

To make this calculation, there shall be considered, on the one hand, the moment of possible suspension or substitution of the sentence pursuant to article 88 and, on the other hand, the date of commission of those offenses that support the finding of habituality.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

###### Article 94 bis.

For the purposes provided for in this Chapter, final sentences of judges or courts imposed in other States of the European Union shall have the same value as those imposed by Spanish judges or courts unless their records have been expunged, or could be so in accordance with Spanish law.

#### TITLE IV

Security measures

##### CHAPTER I

General security measures

###### Article 95.

1. Security measures shall be applied by the Judge or Court, following such reports as it deems appropriate, to persons who find themselves in the situations provided for in the following chapter of this Code, provided that the following circumstances exist:

1.ª That the subject has committed an act defined as a crime.

2.ª That from the act and the personal circumstances of the subject a prognosis of future behavior can be deduced that reveals the likelihood of commission of new crimes.

2. When the penalty that could have been imposed for the crime committed is not custodial, the sentencing judge or court may only order one or more of the measures provided for in Article 96.3.

###### Article 96.

1. The security measures that may be imposed under this Code are custodial and non-custodial.

2. Custodial measures are:

1.ª Confinement in a psychiatric center.

2.ª Confinement in a rehabilitation center.

3.ª Confinement in a special educational center.

3. Non-custodial measures are:

1.ª) Professional disqualification.

2.ª) Expulsion from the national territory of foreigners not legally residing in Spain.

3.ª) Supervised release

4.ª) Family custody. The person subject to this measure shall be subject to the care and supervision of the designated family member who accepts custody, who shall exercise it in relation to the Penitentiary Supervision Judge and without detriment to the educational or work activities of the person in custody.

5.ª) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds.

6.ª) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons.

###### Article 97.

During the execution of the sentence, the sentencing Judge or Court shall adopt, by the procedure established in the following article, one of the following decisions:

- a) Maintain the execution of the imposed security measure.
- b) Decree the termination of any imposed security measure as soon as the criminal dangerousness of the subject disappears.
- c) Replace one security measure with another that it deems more appropriate, among those provided for in the case in question. In the event that the substitution is agreed and LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA the subject evolves unfavorably, the substitution shall be rendered void, and the substituted measure shall be reapplied.
- d) Suspend the execution of the measure in view of the result already obtained with its application, for a period not exceeding that which remains until the maximum specified in the sentence that imposed it. The suspension shall be conditional on the subject not committing an offense during the established period, and may be rendered void if any of the circumstances provided for in Article 95 of this Code are proven again.

###### Article 98.

1. For the purposes of the preceding article, when it concerns a custodial security measure or a supervised release measure that must be executed after the completion of a custodial sentence, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge shall be obliged to submit at least annually a proposal for maintenance, termination, substitution or suspension thereof. In order to formulate such proposal, the Penitentiary Supervision Judge must assess the reports issued by the professionals and specialists who attend the person subject to the security measure or by the competent Public Administrations and, where appropriate, the result of any other actions ordered for this purpose.

2. When it concerns any other non-custodial measure, the sentencing Judge or Court shall directly request from the Administrations, specialists and professionals referred to in the preceding paragraph, the appropriate reports regarding the situation and evolution of the convicted person, their degree of rehabilitation and the prognosis of recidivism or repeated criminal activity.

3. In all cases, the sentencing Judge or Court shall issue a reasoned decision in view of the proposal or reports respectively referred to in the two preceding paragraphs, having heard the person subject to the measure, as well as the Public Prosecutor's Office and the other parties. The victims of the crime who have not appeared as parties shall also be heard when they have so requested at the beginning or at any moment during the execution of the sentence and remain locatable for such purpose.

###### Article 99.

In the case of concurrent sentences and custodial security measures, the judge or court shall order the execution of the measure, which shall be credited toward the sentence. Once the security measure is lifted, the judge or court may, if the execution of the sentence would endanger the effects achieved through it, suspend the execution of the remainder of the sentence for a period not exceeding its duration, or apply any of the measures provided for in Article 96.3.

###### Article 100.

1. The breach of a confinement security measure shall result in the judge or court ordering the readmission of the subject to the same center from which he or she has escaped or to another corresponding to his or her condition.

2. If it concerns other measures, the judge or court may order the substitution of the breached measure by confinement if this is provided for in the case in question and if the breach demonstrates its necessity.

3. In both cases the Judge or Court shall draw up evidence of the breach. For these purposes, the subject's refusal to undergo medical treatment or to continue an initially consented medical treatment shall not be considered a breach of the measure. However, the Judge or Court may order the substitution of the initially or subsequently rejected treatment with another measure from among those applicable to the case in question.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

##### CHAPTER II

Application of security measures

###### Section 1.ª Custodial measures

###### Article 101.

1. To the subject who is declared exempt from criminal responsibility pursuant to number 1.º of Article 20, there may be applied, if necessary, the measure of confinement for medical treatment or special education in an establishment appropriate to the type of anomaly or mental alteration observed, or any other of the measures provided for in paragraph 3 of Article 96. The confinement may not exceed the time that the custodial sentence would have lasted, had the subject been declared responsible, and for this purpose the Judge or Court shall set that maximum limit in the sentence.

2. The person subject to this measure may not leave the establishment without authorization from the sentencing Judge or Court, in accordance with the provisions of Article 97 of this Code.

###### Article 102.

1. To those exempt from criminal responsibility pursuant to number 2.º of Article 20, there shall be applied, if necessary, the measure of confinement in a public rehabilitation center, or a duly accredited or approved private one, or any other of the measures provided for in paragraph 3 of Article 96. The confinement may not exceed the time that the custodial sentence would have lasted, had the subject been declared responsible, and for this purpose the Judge or Court shall set that maximum limit in the sentence.

2. The person subject to this measure may not leave the establishment without authorization from the sentencing Judge or Court in accordance with the provisions of Article 97 of this Code.

###### Article 103.

1. To those who are declared exempt from responsibility pursuant to number 3.º of

###### Article 20

, there may be applied, if necessary, the measure of confinement in a special educational center or any other of the measures provided for in paragraph three of

###### article 96. Confinement may not exceed the time that the sentence would have lasted

of deprivation of liberty, if the subject had been declared responsible and, for this purpose, the Judge or Court shall establish that maximum limit in the sentence.

2. The person subject to this measure may not leave the establishment without authorization from the sentencing Judge or Court in accordance with the provisions of article 97 of this Code.

3. In this case, the proposal referred to in article 98 of this Code must be made at the end of each course or level of education.

###### Article 104.

1. In cases of incomplete exemption in relation to numbers 1, 2 and 3 of

###### article 20

, the Judge or Court may impose, in addition to the corresponding penalty, the measures provided for in articles 101, 102 and 103. However, the measure of confinement shall only be applicable when the penalty imposed is deprivative of liberty and its duration may not exceed that of the penalty provided for by the Code for the offense. For its application, the provisions of article 99 shall be observed.

2. When a measure of confinement among those provided for in the preceding paragraph or in articles 101, 102 and 103 is applied, the sentencing judge or court shall notify the public prosecutor, with sufficient advance notice, of the proximity of its expiration, for the purposes of the provisions of the first additional provision of this Code.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

###### Section 2 On non-custodial measures

###### Article 105.

In the cases provided for in articles 101 to 104, when imposing the custodial measure or during its execution, the Judge or Court may impose with reasoning one or more measures that are listed below. It must also impose one or more of such measures in the other cases expressly provided for in this Code.

1. For a period not exceeding five years:

- a) Supervised release.
- b) Family custody. The person subject to this measure shall be subject to the care and supervision of the family member who is designated and who accepts custody, who shall exercise it in relation to the Supervision Judge and without prejudice to the educational or employment activities of the person in custody.
  - 2. For a period of up to ten years:
- a) Supervised release, when expressly provided by this Code.
- b) Deprivation of the right to possess and carry weapons.
- c) Deprivation of the right to drive motor vehicles and mopeds.

To decree the obligation to observe one or more of the measures provided for in this article, as well as to specify such obligation when required by law to impose them, the sentencing Judge or Court must assess the reports issued by the professionals and specialists in charge of assisting the person subject to the security measure. The Prison Supervision Judge or the services of the corresponding Administration shall inform the sentencing Judge or Court.

In the cases provided for in this article, the sentencing Judge or Court shall order that the competent social assistance services provide the help or attention that is required and legally corresponds to the person subject to non-custodial security measures.

###### Article 106.

1. Supervised release shall consist of subjecting the convicted person to judicial control through compliance on their part with one or more of the following measures:

- a) The obligation to be always locatable by means of electronic devices that allow their permanent monitoring.
- b) The obligation to appear periodically at the place established by the Judge or Court.
- c) The obligation to immediately communicate, within the maximum period and by the means indicated by the Judge or Court for this purpose, each change of place of residence or of place or position of work.
- d) The prohibition to be absent from the place where they reside or from a specific territory without authorization from the Judge or Court.
- e) The prohibition to approach the victim, or those of their family members or other persons determined by the Judge or Court.
- f) The prohibition to communicate with the victim, or with those of their family members or other persons determined by the Judge or Court.
- g) The prohibition to go to certain territories, places or establishments.
- h) The prohibition to reside in certain places.
- i) The prohibition to engage in certain activities that may offer or facilitate the opportunity to commit criminal acts of a similar nature.
- j) The obligation to participate in training, employment, cultural, sexual education or other similar programs.
- k) The obligation to follow external medical treatment, or to undergo periodic medical monitoring.
  - 2. Without prejudice to the provisions of article 105, the Judge or Court must impose in the sentence the measure of supervised release for its compliance subsequent to the custodial penalty imposed whenever this Code so provides expressly.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

In these cases, at least two months before the expiration of the custodial penalty, so that the measure of supervised release can begin at that same time, the Prison Supervision Judge, by the procedure provided for in article 98, shall submit the appropriate proposal to the sentencing Judge or Court, which, in accordance with said procedure, shall specify, without prejudice to the provisions of article 97, the content of the measure by establishing the obligations or prohibitions listed in paragraph 1 of this article that the convicted person must observe.

If the person has been sentenced to several custodial penalties to be served successively, the provisions of the preceding paragraph shall be understood to refer to the moment when the service of all of them is completed.

Likewise, the convicted person upon whom as many measures of supervised release have been imposed for various offenses which, given the content of the obligations or prohibitions established, cannot be executed simultaneously, shall serve them successively, without prejudice to the Judge or Court being able to exercise the powers attributed to it by the following paragraph.

3. By the same procedure of article 98, the Judge or Court may:

- a) Modify henceforth the obligations and prohibitions imposed.
- b) Reduce the duration of supervised release or even terminate it in view of a positive prognosis of reintegration that considers the continuity of the obligations or prohibitions imposed unnecessary or counterproductive.
- c) Cancel the measure when the circumstance described in the preceding letter occurs at the time of specification of the measures regulated in number 2 of this

###### article. 4. In case of non-compliance with one or more obligations the Judge or Court, in view

of the concurrent circumstances and by the same procedure indicated in the preceding numbers, may modify the obligations or prohibitions imposed. If the non-compliance were repeated or serious, revealing the will not to submit to the obligations or prohibitions imposed, the Judge shall also deduce testimony for an alleged offense of

###### article 468 of this Code.

###### Article 107.

The judicial authority may decree with reasoning the measure of disqualification for the exercise of a certain right, profession, trade, industry or commerce, position or employment or other activities, whether or not remunerated, for a period of one to five years, when the person has committed with abuse of said exercise, or in relation to it, a criminal act, and when from the assessment of the concurrent circumstances the danger that they will commit the same offense or similar ones again can be deduced, provided that it is not possible to impose the corresponding penalty because they are in one of the situations provided for in numbers 1, 2 and 3 of article 20.

###### Artículo 108.

1. If the subject is a foreigner not legally resident in Spain, the judge or court shall order in the sentence, after hearing from the person, expulsion from national territory as a substitute for the security measures applicable to them, unless the judge or court, after hearing from the Public Prosecutor's Office, exceptionally and with reasons, considers that the nature of the crime justifies service in Spain.

The expulsion thus ordered shall entail the filing of any administrative procedure whose purpose was authorization to reside or work in Spain. In the event that, once the substitution of the security measure by expulsion has been ordered, this cannot be carried out, the security measure originally imposed shall be served.

2. The foreigner may not return to Spain within a period of 10 years, counted from the date of their expulsion.

3. The foreigner who attempts to breach a judicial decision of expulsion and prohibition of entry referred to in the preceding paragraphs shall be returned by the

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

governmental authority, with the period of prohibition of entry starting to be counted again in its entirety.

#### TÍTULO V

On civil liability derived from crimes and procedural costs

##### CAPÍTULO I

On civil liability and its extent

###### Artículo 109.

1. The commission of an act described by law as a crime creates an obligation to repair, in the terms provided in the laws, the damages and losses caused by it.

2. The injured party may, in any case, choose to claim civil liability before the Civil Jurisdiction.

###### Artículo 110.

The liability established in the preceding article includes: 1.º Restitution.

2.º Reparation of the damage.

3.º Compensation for material and moral losses.

###### Artículo 111.

1. The same property must be returned, whenever possible, with payment for the deterioration and impairment that the judge or court determines. Restitution shall take place even if the property is in the possession of a third party and the third party has acquired it legally and in good faith, reserving their right of recourse against the appropriate party and, where applicable, the right to be compensated by the person civilly liable for the crime.

2. This provision is not applicable when the third party has acquired the property in the manner and with the requirements established by law to make it non-recoverable.

###### Artículo 112.

Reparation of the damage may consist of obligations to give, to do or not to do, which the Judge or Court shall establish taking into account the nature of the damage and the personal and financial circumstances of the guilty party, determining whether they must be fulfilled by the person themselves or may be executed at their expense.

###### Artículo 113.

Compensation for material and moral losses shall include not only those caused to the aggrieved party, but also those inflicted on their relatives or third parties.

###### Artículo 114.

If the victim has contributed through their conduct to the production of the damage or loss suffered, the Judges or Courts may moderate the amount of their reparation or compensation.

###### Artículo 115.

The Judges and Courts, when declaring the existence of civil liability, shall establish with reasoning, in their decisions, the bases on which they base the amount of damages and compensation, and may fix it in the decision itself or at the time of its execution.

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

##### CAPÍTULO II

On persons civilly liable

###### Artículo 116.

1. Every person criminally liable for a crime is also civilly liable if damages or losses arise from the act. If two or more persons are liable for a crime, the judges or courts shall determine the share for which each must be liable.

2. The perpetrators and accomplices, each within their respective class, shall be jointly and severally liable among themselves for their shares, and subsidiarily for those corresponding to the other liable persons.

Subsidiary liability shall be enforced: first, against the assets of the perpetrators, and then, against those of the accomplices.

Both in cases where joint and several liability is enforced and where subsidiary liability is enforced, the right of recourse of the person who has paid against the others for the shares corresponding to each shall be preserved.

3. The criminal liability of a legal person shall entail its civil liability in the terms established in article 110 of this Code jointly and severally with the natural persons who are convicted for the same acts.

###### Artículo 117.

Insurers who have assumed the risk of pecuniary liabilities arising from the use or operation of any property, company, industry or activity, when, as a consequence of an act provided for in this Code, the event that determines the insured risk occurs, shall be directly civilly liable up to the limit of the compensation legally established or agreed by contract, without prejudice to the right of recourse against the appropriate party.

###### Artículo 118.

1. The exemption from criminal liability declared in numbers 1.º, 2.º, 3.º, 5.º and 6.º of article 20, does not include exemption from civil liability, which shall be enforced in accordance with the following rules:

1.ª In the cases of numbers 1.º and 3.º, those who exercise their legal or de facto support are also liable for the acts carried out by those declared exempt from criminal liability, provided there has been fault or negligence on their part and without prejudice to the direct civil liability that may correspond to those not criminally liable.

The Judges or Courts shall equitably graduate the extent to which each of said subjects must be liable with their assets.

2.ª The intoxicated person and the person under the influence of toxic substances are equally liable in the case of number 2.º

3.ª In the case of number 5.º, the persons in whose favor the harm has been prevented shall be directly civilly liable, in proportion to the loss that has been avoided for them, if it is quantifiable or, otherwise, in the amount that the Judge or Court establishes according to their prudent discretion. When the shares for which the interested party must be liable cannot be equitably assigned by the Judge or Court, not even approximately, or when liability extends to the Public Administrations or to the majority of a population and, in any case, whenever the damage has been caused with the consent of the authority or its agents, compensation shall be ordered, where appropriate, in the manner established by special laws and regulations.

4.ª In the case of number 6.º, those who have caused the fear shall be primarily liable, and in their absence, those who have carried out the act.

2. In the case of article 14, the perpetrators of the act shall be civilly liable.

###### Artículo 119.

In all cases of the preceding article, the Judge or Court that issues an acquittal by finding the existence of any of the cited grounds for exemption,

LEGISLACIÓN CONSOLIDADA

shall proceed to fix civil liabilities unless an express reservation of the actions to claim them through the appropriate channel has been made.

###### Article 120.

Those who are also civilly liable, in default of those who are criminally liable: 1.º Curators with full powers of representation who live with the person to whom they provide support, provided there is fault or negligence on their part.

2.º Natural or legal persons who own publishing houses, newspapers, magazines, radio or television stations or any other means of written, spoken or visual dissemination, for crimes committed using the media of which they are owners, without prejudice to the provisions of Article 212.

3.º Natural or legal persons, in cases of crimes committed in establishments of which they are owners, when those who manage or administer them, or their employees or staff, have infringed police regulations or provisions of the authority that are related to the punishable act committed, such that it would not have occurred without such infringement.

4.º Natural or legal persons engaged in any kind of industry or commerce, for crimes committed by their employees or staff, representatives or managers in the performance of their duties or services.

5.º Natural or legal persons who own vehicles capable of creating risks for third parties, for crimes committed in the use thereof by their employees or representatives or authorized persons.

###### Article 121.

The State, the Autonomous Community, the province, the island, the municipality and other public entities, as the case may be, are subsidiarily liable for damages caused by those criminally responsible for intentional or negligent crimes, when these are authority, agents and contractors thereof or public officials in the exercise of their positions or functions, provided that the injury is a direct consequence of the operation of the public services entrusted to them, without prejudice to the patrimonial liability arising from the normal or abnormal operation of such services enforceable in accordance with the rules of administrative procedure, and without in any case there being a duplication of compensation.

If civil liability is claimed in criminal proceedings from the authority, agents and contractors thereof or public officials, the claim must be directed simultaneously against the Administration or public entity allegedly liable as subsidiary civil party.

###### Article 122.

Anyone who has benefited from the effects of a crime by lucrative title is obliged to restore the thing or to compensate for the damage up to the amount of their participation.

##### CHAPTER III

Concerning court costs

###### Article 123.

Court costs are understood to be imposed by law on those criminally responsible for any crime.

###### Article 124.

Costs shall include the fees and compensation arising from judicial proceedings and shall always include the fees of the private prosecution in crimes prosecutable only at the instance of a party.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

##### CHAPTER IV

Concerning the fulfillment of civil liability and other pecuniary liabilities

###### Article 125.

When the assets of the civilly liable party are not sufficient to satisfy all pecuniary liabilities at once, the Judge or Court, after hearing the injured party, may divide payment into installments, determining, according to their prudent discretion and in consideration of the needs of the injured party and the economic possibilities of the liable party, the period and amount of the installments.

###### Article 126.

1. Payments made by the convicted person or the subsidiary civilly liable party shall be applied in the following order:

1.º To the repair of the damage caused and compensation for harm.

2.º To compensation to the State for the amount of expenses incurred on its account in the case.

3.º To the costs of the particular or private prosecutor when their payment is imposed in the sentence. preference among those interested.

5.º To the fine.

2. When the crime has been one of those that can only be prosecuted at the instance of a party, the costs of the private prosecutor shall be satisfied in preference to compensation to the State. The payment of court costs caused to the victim in the cases referred to in Article 14 of the Victims' Rights Act shall have the same preference.

#### TITLE VI

Concerning accessory consequences

###### Article 127.

1. Any penalty imposed for an intentional crime shall entail the loss of the effects arising therefrom and of the assets, means or instruments with which it was prepared or executed, as well as of the gains arising from the crime, whatever transformations they may have undergone.

2. In cases where the law provides for the imposition of a custodial sentence exceeding one year for the commission of a negligent crime, the judge or court may order the loss of the effects arising therefrom and of the assets, means or instruments with which it was prepared or executed, as well as of the gains arising from the crime, whatever transformations they may have undergone.

3. If for any reason the confiscation of the assets indicated in the preceding paragraphs of this article is not possible, the confiscation of other assets shall be ordered for an amount corresponding to their economic value, and to that of the gains that would have been obtained from them. The same procedure shall be followed when the confiscation of specific assets, effects or gains is ordered, but their value is lower than that which they had at the time of their acquisition.

###### Article 127 bis.

1. The judge or court shall also order the confiscation of property, effects and proceeds belonging to a person convicted of any of the following offences when it determines, on the basis of well-founded objective indications, that the property or effects derive from criminal activity, and their lawful origin is not proven:

- a) Offences of trafficking in human beings.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

a bis) Offences of trafficking in organs.

b) Offences relating to prostitution and to sexual exploitation and corruption of minors and offences of abuse and sexual assault of persons under sixteen years of age.

c) Computer-related offences under paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 197 and Article 264.

d) Offences against property and against the socio-economic order in cases of continued criminality and recidivism.

e) Offences relating to punishable insolvencies.

f) Offences against intellectual or industrial property.

g) Offences of corruption in business.

h) Offences of receiving stolen goods under paragraph 2 of Article 298.

i) Offences of money laundering.

j) Offences against the Public Treasury and Social Security.

k) Offences against the rights of workers under Articles 311 to 313.

l) Offences against the rights of foreign nationals.

m) Offences against public health under Articles 368 to 373.

n) Offences of counterfeiting currency.

o) Offences of bribery.

p) Offences of embezzlement.

q) Offences of terrorism.

r) Offences committed within a criminal organization or group.

2. For the purposes of the provisions of paragraph 1 of this Article, the following indications shall be assessed, in particular, among others:

1.º The disproportion between the value of the property and effects in question and the lawful income of the convicted person.

2.º The concealment of ownership or of any power of disposal over the property or effects through the use of interposed natural or legal persons or entities without legal personality, or tax havens or territories with zero taxation that conceal or hinder the determination of the true ownership of the property.

3.º The transfer of the property or effects by means of transactions that hinder or prevent their location or destination and that lack valid legal or economic justification.

3. In these cases, the provisions of paragraph 3 of the preceding Article shall also apply.

4. If subsequently the convicted person is convicted for similar criminal acts committed previously, the judge or court shall assess the scope of the previous confiscation ordered when ruling on confiscation in the new proceedings.

5. The confiscation referred to in this Article shall not be ordered when the criminal activities from which the property or effects derived have prescribed or have already been the subject of criminal proceedings resolved by an acquittal or dismissal order with res judicata effect.

###### Article 127 ter.

1. The judge or court may order the confiscation provided for in the preceding Articles even without a conviction, when the unlawful patrimonial situation is proven in adversarial proceedings and one of the following cases applies:

- a) That the subject has died or suffers from a chronic illness that prevents his or her prosecution and there is a risk that the acts may become time-barred,
- b) he or she is absconding and this prevents the acts from being prosecuted within a reasonable time, or
- c) no penalty is imposed on him or her because he or she is exempt from criminal liability or because it has been extinguished.
  - 2. The confiscation referred to in this Article may only be directed against a person who has been formally charged or against the accused in relation to whom there are reasonable indications of criminality when the situations referred to in the preceding paragraph have prevented the continuation of the criminal proceedings.

CONSOLIDATED LEGISLATION

###### Article 127 quater.

1. Judges and courts may also order the confiscation of the property, effects and proceeds referred to in the preceding Articles that have been transferred to third parties, or of a value equivalent thereto, in the following cases:

- a) In the case of effects and proceeds, when they have acquired them with knowledge that they derive from illicit activity or when a diligent person would have had reason to suspect, in the circumstances of the case, their illicit origin.
